Each `ets` BIF that PBeam runs raises `badarg` with the ERTS `error_info` cause (`id`, `type`, `access`, `already_exists`, `badkey`, `keypos`, `position`, `default`), as ERTS sets it in `erl_db.c`. A table identifier has a marker bit in its reference value, as ERTS marks it with the destructor of its magic binary, so the identifier of a deleted table gives the cause `id` and another reference gives `type`. The bit shows in the number `N2` of the printed reference, as the pid reference marker does, where ERTS keeps it in `N1` below the counter bits (`erl_bif_unique.h:102-105`, `181-186`). The term order moves the table marker to the magic marker bit of `N1`, so it is the ERTS order. Other magic references, such as the references of `atomics:new/2` (`erl_bif_atomics.c:78-88`), have no marker, so their order against a table identifier or an ordinary reference of the same counter block differs from ERTS. `ets:whereis/1` gives the table reference of a named table of each access mode and of each owner, as ERTS does with `DB_INFO`, and `undefined` for a free name. The key position and the index keep only their low 32 bits, as in ERTS. `ets:safe_fixtable/2` gives `true` for `true` and for `false`, and raises `badarg` for another second argument and for a term that is not a table of the run. While a table is fixed, a delete and a clear keep each removed object and its chain node as a pseudo-deleted object, so `ets:next/2` continues after the key, a later insert of the key reuses its node, and the table does not grow or shrink; the last unfix frees the pseudo-deleted objects, and the exit of a process removes its fixations (`erl_db_hash.c:49-60`, `4027-4028`). An `ordered_set` deletes as usual while fixed, as the ERTS tree does, because a walk steps from a key that is not in the table. `ets:info(Tab, fixed)` gives the fixation state, and `safe_fixed` and `safe_fixed_monotonic_time` give `false` for an unfixed table and a runtime error for a fixed table, because PBeam keeps no fixation time. When the key position becomes 0, `ets:insert/2` and `ets:insert_new/2` with a tuple return a runtime error. When the index becomes 0, `ets:lookup_element/4` for a found key returns a runtime error. An exit of the owner deletes its tables and frees their names before the name unregister, the links, and the monitors of the exit. The exit deletes the tables in the order of the ERTS owned list: a table that a trapped `ets:delete/1` of the process frees keeps its position, and a public table of another owner that it deletes goes to the end (`erl_db.c:628-650`, `3031-3051`, `5055-5096`). `ets:match/2` and `ets:match/3` give the matches of ERTS with one scheduler (`erl +S 1`) in the same order: the table keeps the bucket chains of the ERTS hash table, so a traversal reads the same objects in the same order, and a key that hashes as on ERTS gives the same order. With one scheduler, ERTS makes no table with fine locks (`erl_db.c:2656-2657`, `erl_db_util.h:388-389`), so `write_concurrency` does not change the order. ERTS with more schedulers gives a table with `write_concurrency` fine locks, and its traversal steps the buckets by the lock count (`erl_db_hash.c:539-554`, `1023-1050`), so its order is different. A pattern of any depth compiles and matches with a work stack, as `db_match_compile` does. They use the ERTS iterations and reductions, trap after 1000 iterations at a bucket boundary, and raise `badarg` as ERTS does. The `ets:match/3` continuation holds a new ordinary reference where ERTS holds a magic reference to the compiled pattern, and `ets:match/1` returns the runtime error `unresolved external MFA`. When another process changes the table while a match traps, the next turn of the match returns a runtime error. On a table with the key position 0, a tuple pattern matches nothing. Each other `ets` BIF, for example `ets:delete_object/2`, returns the runtime error `unresolved external MFA`. A list insert that takes the table runs the walk of `ets:insert_new/2` and the insert of the objects in steps, one object for each step, and it traps between the objects with the position in the table (`ETS_INSERT_2_LIST_GLOBAL`, `erl_db.c:1927-1949`, `2077-2107`, `2310-2319`). While a list insert traps after its copy, the table is busy, and a process that uses it (`ets:insert/2`, `ets:insert_new/2`, `ets:lookup/2`, `ets:member/2`, `ets:lookup_element/4`, `ets:delete/2`, `ets:match/2`, `ets:match/3`) first runs steps of the walk and the insert under the table lock with the budget of its reductions, and it charges them; its own operation then traps and runs again in a later turn, which helps again while the table stays busy (`handle_lacking_permission`, `erl_db.c:769-798`, `2152-2197`). The inserting process then returns `true` or `false` with no unit left (`erl_db.c:2223-2244`). The copies of the objects of its copy phase are made in one piece in the turn that takes the table, not one object for each unit of the copy phase. When the owner exit deletes the table while the insert traps after its copy, the insert raises `badarg`. The exit of the owner frees each object in the segment of its key hash (row "Scheduling"). A key that holds a pid, a reference, or an external fun hashes the PBeam value, so its object can be in another segment than on ERTS, and the exit can switch out at another segment. A tuple, list, or map key that holds an atom that is not static hashes the PBeam index of that atom (row "Atom table"), with the same result. An atom key hashes its text, so it gives the ERTS segment. | `ets:new/2` also makes `ordered_set` tables (`erl_db.c:2555-2840`). `ets:info/2` gives each item of `table_info` (`erl_db.c:5447-5625`), gives `undefined` for the reference of a deleted table, and raises `badarg` for another reference (`erl_db.c:4544-4550`). When the owner exits, a heir that is alive gets the table and an `ETS-TRANSFER` message (`give_away_to_heir`, `erl_db.c:5076-5080`), and a heir that is the owner changes nothing. The key position 0 aborts the emulator on an insert (status 134, `Invalid tag in make_internal_hash`), and the index 0 for a found key gives a segmentation fault (status 139) on ERTS 29.1.1. A match that traps fixes a table that other processes can write: a delete then only marks the object, and no insert or delete resizes the table until the match ends (`erl_db.c:4026-4031`, `erl_db_hash.c:3690-3692`). `ets:match/1` continues a match from the continuation of `ets:match/3` (`erl_db.c:3766-3769`). With the key position 0, the hash of the pattern key aborts the emulator. The other `ets` BIFs read, change, and delete tables. `ets:delete/1` takes a busy table as ERTS does, so it first completes the insert, and the insert then returns `true` also after the delete (`erl_db.c:2232-2244`, `3021-3029`). An exit of the owner deletes the table with no help and cancels the insert, which then raises `badarg` (`erl_db.c:1755-1763`, `2245-2248`, `5082-5086`). | The start path of #79 reads the application environment from `ac_tab`, a `set` table (`application_controller.erl:544-545`). The logger start of #155 finds, reads, and deletes keys of the `logger` table (`logger_config.erl:45-98`). No start-path code makes another table type or gives a heir. A heir that is the caller needs no table transfer, and no `ets` BIF of PBeam reads the heir. PBeam cannot copy an emulator crash. Each other `ets` BIF comes with its first consumer. (#112, #155) An owner exit deletes the table, and ERTS also raises `badarg` there (`input_probe` `ets_vanish`). On `erl +S 1`, a process that deletes the table with `ets:delete/1` while a 16000-object insert traps after its copy makes the insert return `true`. PBeam does not complete the insert in the deleting process, so `ets:delete/1` returns a runtime error for each table that a trapped list insert uses, also in the check and the copy phase, where ERTS deletes the table and the insert later raises `badarg`. The kernel start deletes no table that another process inserts into. (#234, #698) The pids and the references of ERTS have other values (row "Process and reference counters"), its reference numbers change from run to run, and an external fun hashes the address of its export entry (row "Persistent terms"), so ERTS gives no stable segment for such a key either. The index of an atom that is not static depends on the platform, the build, and the load order (row "Atom table"). (#253) The backlog entry "Complete a trapped `ets:insert/2` in the process that uses the table" of `docs/backlog.md` owns the help of a trapped insert. (#258) `inet_db:init/1` makes a `bag` table (`inet_db.erl:948-951`). The acceptance of #497 needs only the item `type` of `ets:info/2`, so each other item comes with its first consumer. (#497) The causes `id` and `type` need to tell a table identifier from another reference, which ERTS reads from the magic binary of the reference (`tid2tab`, `erl_db.c:280-306`). (#968) `application_controller:get_key_direct/2` and the other start-path readers of `ac_tab` call `ets:match/2` with no concurrent writer during a trap (`application_controller.erl:409-415`). No fixture needs `ets:match/1` yet, so it comes with its first consumer. Table fixation needs a fix record for each process, and no measured path changes a table while a match traps. A key that holds an atom that is not static hashes the PBeam atom index, so its order can differ, as for the owner exit above. (#498)  `ets:select/1,2,3`, `ets:match_object/2`, `ets:next/2`, `ets:prev/2`, `ets:update_element/3,4`, `ets:info/1,2`, `ets:internal_delete_all/2`, and `ets:internal_select_delete/2` run the match-specification dialect of ERTS for `set`, `bag`, and `duplicate_bag` tables, with the ERTS order and reduction charge. A guard function that PBeam does not run yet (`is_record/3`, `is_integer/3`, `binary_part/2,3`, `round/1`, `trunc/1`, `float/1`, `ceil/1`, `floor/1`, and map construction) returns a runtime error that names the function. Each other guard or body that ERTS rejects raises `badarg`. The `stats`, `data`, and `binary` items of `ets:info/2` return a runtime error. The `memory` item counts the PBeam table, in 64-bit words. A run has one scheduler, so `ets:info/2` gives `false` for `write_concurrency` and `decentralized_counters`, as ERTS gives under `+S 1`. `internal_select_delete/2` charges the ERTS reductions but does not yield in the middle of its walk, because PBeam has no trap export for it; another process that uses a busy list-insert table helps the insert and traps, as on ERTS. `internal_delete_all/2` traps between the bucket groups of a fixed table and between the segments of the free that follows the last unfix (`erl_db_hash.c:3007-3051`, `3140-3149`), and a clear of a table that keeps no object runs in one turn. The compiled match specification of an `ets:select/1` continuation stays in the ETS store until its table is deleted, where ERTS frees it with the continuation term. A table that changes while a match or select traps returns a runtime error, because PBeam has no table fixation. (#775)|
| Compiled binary patterns | `binary:compile_pattern/1` gives `{bm, Ref}` for one needle and `{ac, Ref}` for two or more needles (`erl_bif_binary.c:1103-1116`), and `binary:match/2`, `binary:matches/2`, and `binary:split/2,3` take that tuple with the ERTS search results. PBeam keeps the source needles in a store until the run ends. A tuple whose tag and reference do not agree raises `badarg`, as on ERTS (`maybe_binary_match_compile`, `1244-1271`). | ERTS keeps the compiled data in a magic binary and frees it when no term references it (`erts_mk_magic_ref`, `erl_bif_binary.c:1112-1114`). | PBeam does not free a compiled pattern during the run. No measured path compiles many patterns. |
| Packet decoding | `erlang:decode_packet/3` runs the packet types `http_bin` and `httph_bin` with the pinned ERTS length detection and HTTP parser (`packet_parser.c:390-450`, `662-862`) and returns each ERTS result, the `{more, undefined}` case, and each `badarg` case, where an unknown type carries the `badopt` cause. Each other packet type returns the runtime error `unsupported erlang:decode_packet/3 type <Type>`. | `erlang:decode_packet/3` runs each packet type of `packet_parser.c` (`erl_bif_port.c:1476-1617`). | No consumer uses another packet type yet. (#802) |
| Atomic arrays | `atomics:new/2` makes an array of signed or unsigned 64-bit atomics, and `atomics:get/2`, `atomics:put/3`, `atomics:add/3`, and `atomics:info/1` run each operation on it with the ERTS values, `badarg` cases, and `system_limit` case. The preloaded `counters` module computes with them in its default `atomics` mode, so `counters:new/2`, `counters:get/2`, `counters:add/3`, `counters:sub/3`, `counters:put/3`, and `counters:info/1` run as on the pinned ERTS. The `write_concurrency` mode of `counters:new/2` and the `atomics` functions `add_get/3`, `exchange/3`, and `compare_exchange/4` return the runtime error `unresolved external MFA` (row "Unimplemented BIFs"). PBeam keeps each array until the run ends, and an allocation that fails raises `error:system_limit` in the calling process. | ERTS keeps each array in a magic binary and frees it when no term references it (`atomics_destructor`, `erl_bif_atomics.c:49-52`). When the allocation of an array fails, `erts_alloc_n_enomem` ends the emulator (`erl_binary.h:404-407`, `erl_alloc.c:2105-2109`). | The start of the `plug_hello` release calls `counters:new(1, [atomics])`, `counters:get/2`, and `counters:add/3` (`Elixir.Logger.Backends.Internal:init/1`, `Elixir.Logger.Backends.Handler:threshold/1`), and no measured path calls the `write_concurrency` mode or the three other `atomics` functions. |
| Regular expressions | `re:compile/1,2` compile with the PCRE2 10.47 sources of OTP 29.1.1 and returns the ERTS results, messages, and offsets. It raises `badarg` for each call that raises `badarg` on ERTS, and an option error carries the `error_info` cause `badopt` as ERTS does (`erl_bif_re.c:908-912`, `1501-1503`). PBeam keeps each compiled code until the end of the run. The compile stack guard stops at 10 KiB above the stack limit of the calling thread, as in ERTS, so the nesting depth of the error "parentheses are too deeply nested (stack check)" depends on the thread stack and the C build. With `unicode`, a pattern that is not a binary and not chardata, for example `foo`, raises `badarg` through `re:ucompile/2` with the stack trace `[{re, compile, [foo, [unicode]], []}]`, with no location and no caller frames (row "Exception stack trace"). `re:run/2`, `re:run/3`, and `re:internal_run/4` match with the same PCRE2 10.47 sources and give the ERTS values, offsets, error terms, and reduction charge (`erl_bif_re.c:1485-1803`). A `global` run and a `unicode` run that needs the subject conversion trap to `re:grun/3` or `re:urun/3` (`erl_bif_re.c:1530-1534`, `1557-1605`). The trap target resolves through its export entry. A run that has not loaded `re` loads the pinned module through `error_handler:undefined_function/3` and runs `grun/3` and `urun/3` over the `re:internal_run/4` BIF, as ERTS does for a BIF trap to an export that is not loaded (`erts_export_get_or_make_stub`, export.c:228-242; `call_error_handler`, beam_common.c:1294-1342; `error_handler.erl:85-98`). A run with no code server and no init process raises the runtime error `unresolved external MFA re:grun/3` or `re:urun/3`. A match that uses the loops of one turn continues with a fresh budget in the same call, as the trap to `erlang:re_run_trap/3` does (`erl_bif_re.c:1730-1748`, `1852-1866`); PBeam adds each ended turn to the process total and switches the process out only after the call. `re:import/1` decodes a valid export with PCRE2 and re-compiles the source with the options when the encode version, the PCRE2 version, or the architecture differs, as ERTS does (`erl_bif_re.c:703-796`). ERTS passes the encoded binary to `pcre2_serialize_decode` without a length; PBeam checks the serialized header, the character tables, the size of the first code block, and its `code_start` range first, and raises `badarg` for bytes that fail a check. As ERTS does, PBeam trusts the bytecode inside an export with a matching checksum. Each other `re` BIF, for example `re:inspect/2`, returns the runtime error `unresolved external MFA`. | ERTS keeps the compiled code in a magic binary and frees it when no term refers to it (`erl_bif_re.c:117-125`). An option error raises `badarg` with the `error_info` cause `badopt` (`erl_bif_re.c:908-912`, `1501-1503`). The stack check fails at 1687 nested `(?=` groups with the default scheduler stack and at 358 with `+sss 32`. With `+sss 256`, the first error is "regular expression is too complicated" at 1965 groups. With `unicode`, `re:ucompile/2` catches the `badarg` of `unicode:characters_to_binary/2` and raises `badarg` again (`re.erl:1742-1751`). | The start path of #79 compiles the ANSI regular expression of `prim_tty:init/1` (`prim_tty.erl:238`). PBeam has no garbage collection of resources. PBeam does not model the `error_info` of an exception (row "Exception stack trace"). The scheduler stack of ERTS and the Rust thread stack have different sizes. A match that needs more than one turn runs in one step, so other processes do not run during it; each ended turn adds its budget to the process total. An unexpected PCRE2 match error raises `internal_error`; ERTS marks it `EXF_PANIC` (`error.h:133`) and ends the runtime. Each other `re` BIF comes with its first consumer. (#113) |
| Spawn options | `erlang:spawn_opt/4` accepts only the options `link`, `monitor`, `{monitor, MonitorOpts}` with only the `tag` monitor option, `{message_queue_data, Value}`, `{priority, Level}`, and the PBeam option `{capabilities, Map}` (row "Capability checks"). `MonitorOpts` that `erlang:monitor/3` rejects raise `badarg`, and an `alias` or `priority` monitor option returns a runtime error. Any other option returns a runtime error, also when a later option is not valid. A spawn with a monitor returns `{Pid, Reference}`, and the `DOWN` message of the monitor has the tag of the option, else `'DOWN'`. `{message_queue_data, Value}` sets the start value of the `message_queue_data` flag of the new process, and the last such option wins. A value that is not `on_heap` or `off_heap` raises `badarg`. `{priority, Level}` sets the start priority of the new process (row "Scheduling"), else the priority is `normal`, also for the child of a process with another priority. A `Level` that is not `max`, `high`, `normal`, or `low` raises `badarg` (`erl_process.c:12246`, `12275-12287`). | `spawn_opt/4` also accepts each `{monitor, Options}` that `erlang:monitor/3` accepts (`erl_process.c:12406-12418`), `fullsweep_after`, `min_heap_size`, `min_bin_vheap_size`, `max_heap_size`, and `async_dist`. An option that is not valid raises `badarg`, also after an option that PBeam does not support (`erl_process.c:12222-12434`). | `code_server:schedule_on_load/4` spawns the process of an `-on_load` function with `{monitor, [{tag, {'ON_LOAD_DOWN', Module}}]}` (code_server.erl:1395-1396, #708). Only `erlang:spawn_monitor/3` calls `spawn_opt/4` on the other current fixture paths, with `[monitor]`, and `logger_simple_h:adding_handler/1` (`logger_simple_h.erl:39-40`) calls it with `[link, monitor, {message_queue_data, off_heap}]`. (#87, #155) |
| Capability checks | A run with no policy, `BeamProgram::execute` and the `pbeam` binary without `--policy` or with `--no-capability-checks`, skips each capability check, as if each entry were `ambient`. The launcher sets this option before the root process starts, and no BIF can change it. No process gets a capability copy, so the run makes no delegation record. The `{capabilities, Map}` spawn option raises `badarg` for a `Map` that is not valid, but it does not reduce the child. `pbeam_cap:revoke/2` and `pbeam_cap:policy_revoke/2` return `ok` after their argument checks, and `pbeam_cap:request/2` returns `ok` after the check of the kind; all three have no effect. Thus each permitted operation gives the ERTS result. `pbeam` rejects `--no-capability-checks` with `--policy` as a usage error. In a run with a policy, each process holds copies of capabilities. `BeamProgram::execute_with_policy` takes a Rust `Policy`, for example `AllowAll` for tests: a delegable `process`, `terminal`, `port`, `env`, `signal`, `runtime`, `persistent_term`, and `code` capability with all operations and no constraint. With this policy each spawn behaves as on ERTS. `erlang:spawn/3`, `erlang:spawn_link/3`, and `erlang:spawn_opt/4` check the `process` capability of the caller after the argument checks and before they take a pid or a reference: `spawn` for the module of the MFA, `link` for a link, and `monitor` for a monitor. A denied check raises `error:capability_denied`. The `modules` constraint compares only the module of the MFA that the BIF gets. Thus a fun that `erlang:spawn/1` starts is checked as `erlang` (`erlang:apply/2`), and an OTP start through `proc_lib` is checked as `proc_lib`. A child gets a copy of each delegable capability of its parent. The option `{capabilities, Map}` gives the child only the kinds that `Map` names, each reduced: `Map` maps a kind atom to a map with the optional keys `operations` (a list of operation atoms), `delegable` (`true` or `false`), and the constraint field of the kind (`modules` and `signals` take atoms, `executables` and `names` take strings, each also `any`). An omitted key keeps the value of the parent copy, and the last `{capabilities, Map}` option wins. A request for a kind that the parent cannot delegate, or for more operations or names than the parent copy permits, raises `error:capability_denied`, and no process starts. A `Map` that is not valid raises `badarg`. These entries also check a capability of the caller after their argument checks and before their first effect, and a denied call raises `error:capability_denied` and has no effect: `erlang:open_port/2` (`port` `spawn`), `erlang:port_command/2` (`port` `command`), `erlang:port_close/1` (`port` `close`), and `erlang:port_info/1` (`port` `inspect`) for an open port, also of another process, with the `executables` constraint for the program name as the caller gives it, with no path resolution, so a closed port and an atom give `undefined` with no check; `os:getenv/1` (`env` `read`), with the `names` constraint for the native bytes of the name; `os:set_signal/2` (`signal` `ignore` for `ignore`, `handle` for `handle`, and both for `default`), with the `signals` constraint for the signal atom; and the `prim_tty` bindings `write_nif/2` (`terminal` `write`), `read_nif/3` and `tty_select/2` (`terminal` `read`), `tty_init/2` (`terminal` `raw_mode`), `tty_window_size/1` (`terminal` `window_size`), `setupterm_nif/0` and `setlocale/1` (`terminal` `setup`), which read no argument, so they check first, and `isatty/1` and `tty_create/1` (`terminal` `query`) after the fd check; `erlang:halt/2` (`runtime` `halt`), also for `abort` and a crash dump slogan; `erlang:system_flag/2` (`runtime` `system_flag`), for each item and value that the value check of row "System flags" accepts, so a value that only the missing subsystem rejects, such as a scheduler count above the host count, raises `capability_denied` in a denied process where ERTS raises `badarg`; `erlang:load_nif/2` from module code (`runtime` `load_nif`), after the path check and before the library state; `erlang:link/1` (`process` `link`) and `erlang:monitor/2,3` (`process` `monitor`), also for the pid of the caller; `erlang:exit/2` (`process` `exit_other`) for a signal to another process, also through its alias, and for a port, but not for a signal to the caller; and the `send` instruction, `erlang:send/2`, `erlang:send/3`, and `erlang:start_timer/3` (`process` `send`), also for the caller; and `persistent_term:get/0,1,2` (`persistent_term` `read`) and `persistent_term:put/2` (`persistent_term` `write`), which have no argument check, so a denied `persistent_term:get/1` of a key with no value raises `error:capability_denied`, not `badarg`, and a denied put of the same value again also raises it; `erlang:process_info/2`, `erlang:is_process_alive/1`, and `erts_internal:is_process_alive/2` (`process` `inspect`), and `erts_internal:group_leader/2,3` (`process` `group_leader`), only for another process, after the argument checks and before the process lookup, so a denied call to a pid that no process has raises `error:capability_denied`, not `undefined`, `false`, or `badarg`, and a denied request sends no signal and no reply; `erlang:group_leader/2` checks through them; `erlang:finish_loading/1` (`code` `load`) checks for each module of the list of prepared code, after the argument checks and before any module loads, so a denied call loads no module, and an empty list needs the operation and compares no module name; `erlang:module_loaded/1` and `erlang:get_module_info/2` (`code` `inspect`) check for the module name, after the atom checks of the module and the key and before the code table lookup, so a denied call does not tell whether a module is loaded, with the `modules` constraint. The trap exports of `persistent_term:get/0` and `persistent_term:put/2` do not check again. The three sends share one path, which checks once after the check of the destination form and before the name lookup, so a denied send to a name that no process has raises `error:capability_denied`, not `badarg`. An entry checks before the runtime error of a part that PBeam does not support, such as a port or a `time_offset` monitor. A call of `erlang:load_nif/2` through `apply` has no check: it only gives `{error, {bad_lib, Text}}`. The `runtime` and `persistent_term` kinds have no constraint field. A denied `write_nif/2` raises at once, with no dirty IO call. A `names` or `executables` name is compared as its native bytes in the file name encoding of the host. In boot mode, the IO service entry checks `terminal` `write` of the sender of an output request to `user` or `standard_error` (row "Boot authority"). The column "Capability" of `docs/effect-inventory.md` lists each check. The entries marked `ambient` in that column have no check by decision, so a process with no capability gets the ERTS result. The entries marked `later` have no check yet, so a process with no capability also gets the ERTS result: #465 adds the checks of `erlang:module_loaded/1` and `erlang:finish_loading/1`, and the planned `file` kind of `docs/capability-security-migration.md` adds the check of `prim_file:read_file_nif/1`, `list_dir_nif/1`, `read_link_nif/1`, and `get_cwd_nif/0`, so each process can read each host file, directory, and symbolic link that the OS process can read. It also adds the check of `prim_file:open_nif/2`, `close_nif/1`, `read_nif/2`, `write_nif/2`, and `seek_nif/3`, so each process can open, read, write, and create each host file that the OS process can (#731). `prim_file:read_info_nif/2` is `ambient` until the planned `file` kind of `docs/capability-security-migration.md` adds its check (#390), so each process can read the information of each host file. Each copy has an identifier, and a delegation record of the run keeps its holder, its source (the launcher or the copy of the parent), and the copies that went from it to children. A revocation is a PBeam signal in the signal queue (row "Signal queue"). When the holder handles it, the holder deletes its copy and sends a revocation signal to each downstream copy, so the next check of the holder and of each downstream process after its signal is denied. The revocation does not end a holder. When a process leaves the process table, it releases its copies; the record of a copy with receivers stays until its last receiver goes, and a revocation goes through it at once to the copies below. Before each scheduling step, the launcher revokes the initial copies of the root process of each kind that `Policy::revocations` gives; the default revokes nothing. `pbeam_cap:revoke(Pid, Kind)` revokes the copies of the capability kind `Kind` that the caller gave to `Pid` at spawn and returns `ok`. When the caller gave `Pid` no copy of `Kind` that has a record, also after `Pid` exited with no receivers, it raises `error:capability_denied`. A `Pid` that is not a pid and a `Kind` that is not a kind atom raise `badarg`. The call costs 1 reduction, as a light BIF. The policy process revokes with `pbeam_cap:policy_revoke/2` (row "Policy module"). | ERTS has no capabilities. Each spawn that passes the argument checks starts a process, and `{capabilities, Map}` is an option that is not valid, so it raises `badarg` (`erl_process.c:12222-12434`, `bif.c:1039-1051`). Each other entry has the effect after its argument checks (`erl_bif_port.c:60-130`, `168-261`, `384-432`, `486-540`; `erl_bif_os.c:94-110`, `145-160`; `prim_tty_nif.c:257-285`, `354-427`, `429-675`, `747-791`, `988-1072`, `1074-1233`; `bif.c:4316-4389`, `4972-5304`; `erl_nif.c:4730-4787`; `bif.c:136-186`, `758-997`, `1721-1870`, `2458-2908`; `erl_hl_timer.c:1804-1830`, `2605-2620`; `erl_bif_persistent.c:314-398`, `642-700`; `erl_bif_info.c:1273-1502`, `4049-4103`; `bif.c:4898-4970`). ERTS has no module `pbeam_cap`, so `pbeam_cap:revoke/2` raises `error:undef` (probe: `revoke_probe:cascade/0` gives `{[ok,ok],[{error,undef},{error,undef}],[ok,ok,ok],[true,true]}` with `erl +S 1`). | Transition 2 of `docs/capability-security-migration.md` adds process authority. A permitted operation keeps the ERTS result, and a run with no policy permits each operation. (#334, #335, #336, #368, #369, #370, #371, #372, #373, #437, #443, #463, #465) |
| Policy module | `BeamProgram::execute_with_policy_module` and `pbeam --policy Module` run the root process with a policy module: a loaded BEAM module in any BEAM language that exports `initial/1` and `grant/3`. Before the root process, the launcher makes the policy process, `<0.1.0>`. It has no parent and no link, so no supervisor tree holds it. It holds the capabilities of `AllowAll`, so the effects of the policy code have no other check. Its first call is `initial(#{pid => <0.0.0>, parent => none, initial_call => {M, F, A}})`, or in boot mode the call of `pbeam_cap:enter_runtime/2` (row "Boot authority"). The root process gets the capabilities of the list of capability maps that the call returns. Each map has the keys `kind`, `operations`, `delegable`, and the constraint field of the kind, with the values of the `{capabilities, Map}` option. Another value, an exception that the call does not catch, a call of more than 100000 reductions, and an exit of the policy process stop the run with the error `the policy denied the root process: <cause>`. Without boot mode, the root process does not start. In boot mode, the root process runs before the call, with the boot authority, and the error stops the run while the root process waits in `pbeam_cap:enter_runtime/2` (row "Boot authority"). `pbeam_cap:request(Kind, Request)` adds a request to the queue of the policy process, and the caller waits. The policy process calls `grant(Kind, Request, #{pid => Pid, parent => Parent, initial_call => {M, F, A}})` for each request in order, and keeps its state between the calls. The value `{grant, Capability}` sends a grant signal that adds the capability to the caller, with the source `policy`, and the call returns `ok`. Each other value, an exception that the call does not catch, and more than 100000 reductions deny the request: the call raises `error:capability_denied`. A `Kind` that is not a kind atom raises `badarg`. A run with a Rust policy has no policy process, so the call raises `error:capability_denied` at once. So does a call after the exit of the policy process, and a call of the policy process itself. A run with no policy returns `ok` with no request (row "Capability checks"). A call of the policy code that waits for a message has no time limit, and its requesters wait with it. The launcher does not start the policy process again. No implemented BIF gives the pid of the policy process to a guest, because `erlang:list_to_pid/1` and `erlang:binary_to_term/1` are not implemented, and `erlang:processes/0` gives a process that is not a boot process a list without the policy process. In a call of the policy code, `pbeam_cap:policy_revoke(Pid, Kind)` revokes the copies of the capability kind `Kind` of the process `Pid`, whatever their source (the launcher, the policy, or a parent), and returns `ok`. Each copy that `Pid` holds gets a revocation signal from the policy process, which cascades as the signal of `pbeam_cap:revoke/2` does (row "Capability checks"), so the next check of `Pid` and of each downstream process after its signal is denied. After `Pid` exited, its released records send the revocation at once to the live copies below them. A `Pid` with no copy of `Kind` and no released record of it gives `ok` with no effect. A grant signal that `Pid` did not handle yet has no record, so the call does not revoke its copy. Another caller than the policy process raises `error:capability_denied`. A `Pid` that is not a pid and a `Kind` that is not a kind atom raise `badarg` before the check of the caller. The call costs 1 reduction, as a light BIF. The policy process publishes no shared policy table yet. | ERTS has no policy module, no policy process, and no module `pbeam_cap`, so `pbeam_cap:request/2` and `pbeam_cap:policy_revoke/2` raise `error:undef` (probe: `policy_probe:granted/0` gives `{string,{error,undef},[string,string]}`, and `policy_probe:revoke_denied/0` gives nine `{error,undef}` results, then `string` and `pid`, with `erl +S 1`). | Transition 2 of `docs/capability-security-migration.md`: the policy decides grants in BEAM code at grant time, and a check reads only the copies of the caller. A permitted operation keeps the ERTS result. Evidence: the example `restricted_guest` in boot mode with its strict Elixir policy `RestrictedGuest.Policy`, which gives only the capabilities that the guest uses, gives the stdout, the stderr, and the status of its ERTS run (`tests/feature/example.rs`). (#337, #338, #378, #463) |
| Boot authority | `pbeam --boot --policy Module` and `BeamProgram::execute_with_policy_module` with `boot` set to `true` run in boot mode, only with a policy module. The launcher makes the policy process and then the root process at once, without `initial/1`. The root process starts with the boot authority: each check passes without copies, the process has no delegation record, and `pbeam_cap:policy_revoke/2` of it returns `ok` with no effect. A process that a boot process spawns without `{capabilities, Map}` is a boot process; with the option, the spawn raises `error:capability_denied`. In a boot process, `pbeam_cap:request/2` returns `ok` after the check of the kind and does not call the policy. `pbeam_cap:enter_runtime(Module, Function)` is a PBeam BIF. In the root process in boot mode, it adds the request of `initial(#{pid => <0.0.0>, parent => none, initial_call => {Module, Function, 0}})` to the queue of the policy process and waits. The value replaces the boot authority of the root process with its capabilities, with the source `launcher`, one way, and the call returns `ok`. A denial stops the run with the error `the policy denied the root process: <cause>` of a run without boot mode. A `Module` or a `Function` that is not an atom raises `badarg`. A process that is not a boot process, also in a run with no policy, gets `ok` with no effect. A boot process that is not the root process gets the runtime error `unsupported pbeam_cap:enter_runtime/2 in a boot process that is not the root process`. `pbeam_boot` calls `pbeam_cap:enter_runtime(Module, Function)` before `Module:Function()` (row "Boot"). The runtime to boot gate: after its capability check, a process that is not a boot process gets `error:capability_denied` for `erlang:send/2,3` and the `send` instruction to a boot process by pid or registered name, `erlang:start_timer/3` for a boot process or a name that a boot process has when the timer starts, `erlang:link/1`, `erlang:exit/2` also through an alias of a boot process, `erts_internal:group_leader/2,3`, which `erlang:group_leader/2` calls, for a boot target, `erlang:process_info/2`, `erlang:is_process_alive/1`, and `erts_internal:is_process_alive/2` for a boot process, and `erlang:unregister/1` of the name of a boot process. The call has no effect. A monitor of a boot process and a message to an alias of a boot process pass. A boot process has no gate. Thus a `gen:call/4` of a boot process to a runtime process with the timeout `infinity` gets no reply: `gen:reply/2` sends the reply to the pid of the caller and catches the denial (measured with a scratch probe; with a timeout of 1000 ms the reply goes through the alias and arrives). The gate reads the boot flag of the target, so a denied call tells whether a live process is a boot process. `pbeam_cap:serve(Pid, io)` is a PBeam BIF. In a boot process, it registers the live boot process `Pid` as an IO service, by its pid, and returns `ok`; another `Pid` raises `badarg`. A `Pid` that is not a pid and a second argument that is not `io` raise `badarg`. A process that is not a boot process, also in a run with no policy, gets `ok` with no effect. `pbeam_boot` registers `user` and `standard_error` (row "Boot"). The IO service entry: the gate permits a message from a runtime process to an IO service only when it is `{io_request, From, ReplyAs, Request}` with `From` equal to the sender, and `Request` is `{put_chars, Encoding, Chars}`, `{put_chars, Encoding, io_lib, Function, Arguments}` with `Function` `format`, `fwrite`, or `write` and no fun at any depth in `Arguments`, or `{requests, Requests}` with a proper list of such requests, also nested and also empty. Each `put_chars` request needs the `terminal` `write` capability of the sender when the message is sent. Each other message raises `error:capability_denied` and is not sent, for example `{get_line, Encoding, Prompt}`, `{setopts, Options}`, an MFA of another module or function, and a request with a false `From`. Thus `IO.puts/1` raises `error:capability_denied` in a sender without `terminal` `write`, also after a revocation, and a restricted guest that prints a fun with `io:format/2`, such as `io:format("~p", [Fun])`, gets `error:capability_denied` where ERTS prints the fun. A request that is in the queue of the IO server when the revocation arrives is not withdrawn. | ERTS has no capabilities, no boot authority, and no module `pbeam_cap`, so `pbeam_cap:enter_runtime/2` raises `error:undef` (`mise run erts-eval 'pbeam_cap:enter_runtime(boot_authority_probe, guest)'` gives `exception error: undef`). Each process can send to, link to, signal, inspect, and unregister each other process (`unregister_1` in `erts/emulator/beam/bif.c:2324-2335` at `OTP-29.1.1`). A `gen:call/4` with the timeout `infinity` to a local pid gets its reply at the pid of the caller (`lib/stdlib/src/gen.erl:233-250`); with another timeout, at an alias (`lib/stdlib/src/gen.erl:251-262`). `gen:reply/2` catches an exception of the send to a pid (`lib/stdlib/src/gen.erl:553-554`). `io` sends `{io_request, self(), Mref, Request}` and does not catch an exception of the send (`lib/stdlib/src/io.erl:1666-1685`); `io:format/2`, `io:fwrite/2`, and `io:write/1` send the `io_lib` MFA form (`lib/stdlib/src/io.erl:1750-1755`). `group` and `standard_error` apply the MFA of a `put_chars` request in their own process (`lib/kernel/src/group.erl:626-627`, `lib/kernel/src/standard_error.erl:120-121`). `io_lib:format/2` calls the fun of the `~K` modifier (`lib/stdlib/src/io_lib_format.erl:247-248`), and `io_lib:write/2` calls the fun of the option `{maps_order, Fun}` (`lib/stdlib/src/io_lib.erl:599-604`): with `erl -noshell`, `io:format(user, "~Kp~n", [Fun, Map])` and the request `{put_chars, unicode, io_lib, write, [Map, [{maps_order, Fun}]]}` to `user` call `Fun` in the process of `user` (measured with `mise run erts-eval`). | Owner decision of 2026-10-11 (Suikou review 01a1282e): capabilities apply only to runtime processes, and the boot is not under the policy. This is the interim one-way drop, option B. A permitted operation keeps the ERTS result. The gate denies by default, because an unadapted privileged service is not available to a secure guest (Transition 4 of `docs/capability-security-migration.md`). The IO service entry prevents a confused deputy: the IO server writes for a runtime process only with the `terminal` authority of that process, and it does not apply an MFA or call a fun of the guest with the boot authority. Evidence: `boot_authority_probe` of `fixtures/policy` with `boot_policy` (`tests/feature/policy_option.rs`), and `restricted_guest_probe` with `restricted_guest_probe_policy` (`tests/feature/example.rs`). (#462, #463) |
| Monitors | `erlang:monitor/2` and `erlang:monitor/3` monitor a process by pid, by registered name, and by `{Name, nonode@nohost}`, and a port by name, as ERTS does. The options `{alias, Mode}` and `{tag, Tag}` work as on ERTS. A port name always gives a `noproc` monitor down signal to the caller, because PBeam registers no port. A monitor of the type `port` with a port sends `{'DOWN', Ref, port, Port, Reason}` with the exit reason of the port when the port closes, and `noproc` at once for a port that is not open, as ERTS does (io.c:3922-3936). `erlang:monitor(time_offset, clock_service)` returns the runtime error `unsupported time_offset monitor`. The option `priority` returns the runtime error `unsupported monitor option priority` when the call makes a monitor. | `monitor(time_offset, clock_service)` returns a reference, and the process gets a `CHANGE` message when the time offset changes (`bif.c:952-969`). The option `priority` makes the `DOWN` message a priority message, which goes before the ordinary messages (`bif.c:711-713`). | PBeam has no time offset service and no priority messages. No measured path makes a `time_offset` monitor or uses the `priority` option. (#153) |
| External term format | `erlang:term_to_binary/1,2` encodes each term type that PBeam holds with the tags of `external.c`, a bitstring that is not a whole number of bytes as `BIT_BINARY_EXT` with its unused tail bits cleared (`external.c:3861`, `3916-3935`), with the options `compressed`, `{compressed, L}`, `{minor_version, V}`, `deterministic`, and `local`, and `erlang:external_size/1` gives its size. A match context, a record, and a pid reference give a runtime error. A local fun encodes as `NEW_FUN_EXT` with the module MD5, the index, the old index, and the old unique value of its fun entry and the creator `<0.0.0>` (`external.c:3966-3993`). `erlang:binary_to_term/1,2` decodes the tags of `external.c` except `FUN_EXT` and `RECORD_EXT`, which give a runtime error, and an `EXPORT_EXT` under `safe` gives a runtime error because PBeam does not check that the export is active. A decoded `NEW_FUN_EXT` takes the fun entry of the loaded code with the same module, MD5, index, and arity, else a new stub entry with the decoded old index and old unique value (`external.c:5102-5176`, `erl_fun.c:299-309`). PBeam keeps no node table of stub entries: ERTS gives each later decode of the same key, and the code that loads later with that key, the first stub entry with its old values, and PBeam does not. A pid, port, or reference of another node, a reference of another scheduler, a pid reference, a reference with the high bit of its third number, a fun with an external arity above 2^32 - 1, and a map with a key that PBeam cannot hold give a runtime error. The reductions of the two BIFs differ: `term_to_binary/1,2` charges 1 reduction for each call and `binary_to_term/1,2` charges none, where ERTS charges by the size of the term (`external.c:2520-2530`, `2070-2120`). `erlang:external_size/2`, `erlang:term_to_iovec/1,2`, and `erlang:read_timer/2` are not supported. | ERTS encodes and decodes each term and charges reductions in steps of the term size (`external.c:2449-2600`, `4270-5340`). | The Plug serve path needs the session, config, and logger terms; funs, records, and bitstrings are not on it. (#769) |
| Instruction calls | An external call to `erlang:apply/2` or `erlang:apply/3` runs as the ERTS `i_apply_fun` or `i_apply` instruction. A `call_ext`, `call_ext_last`, or `call_ext_only` of `erlang:load_nif/2` runs as the ERTS `i_load_nif` instruction (see "NIF stubs"). A `call_ext`, `call_ext_last`, or `call_ext_only` of `erlang:yield/0` runs as the ERTS `i_yield` instruction. The `apply` and `apply_last` instructions call the function through the same path as `call_ext`, so an `apply` of `erlang:apply/2` or `erlang:apply/3` runs that apply, and an `apply` of `erlang:yield/0` runs its body in `erlang.beam`, as in ERTS. | The loader turns these calls into the `i_apply_fun`, `i_apply`, `i_load_nif`, and `i_yield` instructions (`ops.tab`). For `erlang:load_nif/2` and `erlang:yield/0`, only the `call_ext`, `call_ext_last`, and `call_ext_only` forms change (`jit/arm/ops.tab:615-623`, `664-666`; `emu/ops.tab:854-862`). They call the function or let other processes run. `apply` and `apply_last` with `erlang:apply/2` or `erlang:apply/3` run that apply (`fixed_apply` in `beam_common.c`). | `preempt_probe:yields/0` and `preempt_probe:reductions/0` of `fixtures/preempt` call `erlang:yield/0` through `call_ext`, `call_ext_last`, `call_ext_only`, and `apply/3`, with the same results as `erl +S 1` (#230). #109 adds `erlang:apply/2` and `erlang:apply/3`. #127 adds `erlang:load_nif/2`. (#87, #76, #109, #127) |
| NIF stubs | PBeam loads only its static NIF libraries through `erlang:load_nif/2`, and it links only the functions that this row lists. It links `crypto:aead_cipher_nif/7`, `crypto:cipher_algorithms/0`, `crypto:cipher_info_nif/1`, `crypto:do_exor/2`, `crypto:ng_crypto_one_time_nif/5`, `crypto:pkey_sign_nif/5`, `crypto:pkey_sign_heavy_nif/5`, `crypto:pkey_verify_nif/6`, `crypto:curve_algorithms/0`, `crypto:enable_fips_mode_nif/1`, `crypto:hash_algorithms/0`, `crypto:hash_equals_nif/2`, `crypto:hash_final_nif/1`, `crypto:hash_final_xof_nif/2`, `crypto:hash_info/1`, `crypto:hash_init_nif/1`, `crypto:hash_nif/2`, `crypto:hash_update_nif/2`, `crypto:info_fips/0`, `crypto:info_lib/0`, `crypto:info_nif/0`, `crypto:kem_algorithms_nif/0`, `crypto:mac_algorithms/0`, `crypto:mac_final_nif/1`, `crypto:mac_init_nif/3`, `crypto:mac_nif/4`, `crypto:mac_update_nif/2`, `crypto:pubkey_algorithms/0`, `crypto:rsa_opts_algorithms/0`, `crypto:pbkdf2_hmac_nif/5`, `crypto:strong_rand_bytes_nif/1`, `crypto:strong_rand_range_nif/1`, `prim_buffer:new/0`, `prim_buffer:size/1`, `prim_buffer:peek_head/1`, `prim_buffer:copying_read/2`, `prim_buffer:write/2`, `prim_buffer:skip/2`, `prim_buffer:find_byte_index/2`, `prim_buffer:try_lock/1`, `prim_buffer:unlock/1`, `prim_file:open_nif/2`, `prim_file:close_nif/1`, `prim_file:read_nif/2`, `prim_file:write_nif/2`, `prim_file:seek_nif/3`, `prim_file:read_info_nif/2`, `prim_file:read_file_nif/1`, `prim_file:list_dir_nif/1`, `prim_file:read_link_nif/1`, `prim_file:get_cwd_nif/0`, `prim_file:altname_nif/1`, `prim_file:del_dir_nif/1`, `prim_file:del_file_nif/1`, `prim_file:make_dir_nif/1`, `prim_file:make_hard_link_nif/2`, `prim_file:make_soft_link_nif/2`, `prim_file:rename_nif/2`, `prim_file:set_cwd_nif/1`, `prim_file:set_owner_nif/3`, `prim_file:set_permissions_nif/2`, `prim_file:set_time_nif/4`, `prim_tty:isatty/1`, `prim_tty:tty_create/1`, `prim_tty:tty_init/2`, `prim_tty:tty_select/2`, `prim_tty:tty_window_size/1`, `prim_tty:tty_encoding/1`, `prim_tty:setlocale/1`, `prim_tty:setupterm_nif/0`, `prim_tty:tigetnum_nif/1`, `prim_tty:tigetflag_nif/1`, `prim_tty:write_nif/2`, `prim_tty:read_nif/3`, `zlib:close_nif/1`, `zlib:open_nif/0`, `zlib:deflateInit_nif/6`, `zlib:deflateReset_nif/1`, `zlib:deflateEnd_nif/1`, `zlib:deflate_nif/4`, `zlib:inflateInit_nif/3`, `zlib:inflateReset_nif/1`, `zlib:inflateEnd_nif/1`, `zlib:inflate_nif/4`, `zlib:getStash_nif/1`, `zlib:clearStash_nif/1`, `zlib:setStash_nif/2`, `zlib:set_controller_nif/2`, `zlib:enqueue_nif/2`, `zstd:init_compress_nif/0`, `zstd:init_decompress_nif/0`, `zstd:set_compress_parameter_nif/3`, `zstd:set_pledged_src_size_nif/2`, `zstd:set_decompress_parameter_nif/3`, `zstd:compress_stream_nif/3`, `zstd:decompress_stream_nif/3`, `zstd:compress_reset_nif/1`, `zstd:decompress_reset_nif/1`, `zstd:compress_close_nif/1`, `zstd:decompress_close_nif/1`, `asn1rt_nif:encode_per_complete/1`, `asn1rt_nif:decode_ber_tlv_raw/1`, and `asn1rt_nif:encode_ber_tlv/1` as native bindings that are active when the module loads, and each entry to one of these functions runs its binding in place of the BEAM stub. `isatty/1` gives the ERTS result for fd 0, 1, or 2 of the host. `tty_create/1` keeps a TTY resource with the output fd and its first mode by reference in the `Ttys` value of the node `Resources`, next to the compiled code of `re:compile/2` (#113). PBeam keeps each TTY resource until the end of the run. `tty_init/2` gives the ERTS result and sets the ERTS raw or first mode on the output fd. It sends a monitor signal to the caller, as `enif_monitor_process` does (erl_nif.c:3957-3989), and when the caller exits while it is still the owner of the resource, PBeam sets fd 0 to the first mode of the resource, as `tty_monitor_down` does (prim_tty_nif.c:1235-1241). `tty_select/2` raises `error:badarg` for a term that is not a TTY resource. Else it selects fd 0 for read with its second argument as the message reference, makes the caller the reader of the resource, monitors the caller as `tty_init/2` does, and returns `ok` (see "Input select"). `read_nif/3` raises `error:badarg` for a term that is not a TTY resource, then for a size that is not an integer from 0 to 2^64 - 1. Else it reads at most 1024 bytes from fd 0 with one `read` and gives the ERTS result (prim_tty_nif.c:429-675): `{error, closed}` for 0 bytes, `{ok, <<>>}` for `EAGAIN` and `EINTR`, `{error, {read, Errno}}` with the ERTS `erl_errno_id` atom for each other failure, and else `{ok, Bytes}`. Each `{ok, _}` selects fd 0 again for the caller. ERTS runs `read_nif/3` on a dirty IO scheduler (prim_tty_nif.c:163), so a `read` that blocks stops all PBeam processes until it returns (see "Scheduling"). The `prim_tty` reader calls it only after a select message, when fd 0 has input. `tty_encoding/1` gives `utf8` for each term, as ERTS does on Unix (prim_tty_nif.c:287-297). `tty_window_size/1` gives the ERTS result for the window size of fd 0 or of the output fd. `setlocale/1` sets the `LC_CTYPE` locale from the environment and gives the ERTS result for its codeset. `setupterm_nif/0` reads the terminfo entry with the host curses library that the pinned ERTS links (`libncurses` on macOS, `libtinfo.so.6` on Linux), and a failure gives `{error, {setupterm, Errno}}` with the ERTS `erl_errno_id` atom. `tigetnum_nif/1` and `tigetflag_nif/1` raise `error:badarg` for a term that is not iodata. Else they give the result of `tgetnum` and `tgetflag` of the same curses library for the bytes of the iodata up to the first NUL, as ERTS does (prim_tty_nif.c:841-863). For iodata with no NUL, PBeam uses all its bytes as the code. `write_nif/2` raises `error:badarg` for a term that is not a TTY resource. Else it writes each group of 1024 elements of the iovec to the output fd with `writev`, calls `writev` again on `EINTR` and `EAGAIN`, writes the remaining bytes after a partial write, and gives `{error, Errno}` with the ERTS `erl_errno_id` atom for a failed write, as ERTS does (prim_tty_nif.c:354-427). It raises `error:badarg` for an iovec that is not a list, for a group with an element that is not a binary, or for the group that holds the last element of an improper list, with any tail, after it writes the earlier groups (`examine_iovec_term`, erl_nif.c:4092-4150). ERTS runs `write_nif/2` on a dirty IO scheduler (prim_tty_nif.c:161), and PBeam runs it on a dirty IO thread, so a `writev` that waits stops only its caller (see "Dirty NIFs"). `prim_file:read_info_nif/2` raises `error:badarg` for a follow flag that is not an integer in the C `int` range. It gives `{error, einval}` for a path that is not a binary whose bytes after its first NUL byte are all NUL, also for `<<>>`. Else it reads the bytes before the first NUL with `stat`, or with `lstat` for the flag 0, and then `access` with `R_OK` and `W_OK`. It gives the ERTS `#file_info` tuple, with the times limited to 1902-01-01 to 9999-12-31 and the mode, link count, device numbers, inode, user, and group cut to their low 32 bits, or `{error, Errno}` with the ERTS `erl_errno_id` atom (prim_file_nif.c:996-1038, unix_prim_file.c:71-101, 712-769). ERTS runs `read_info_nif/2` on a dirty IO scheduler (prim_file_nif.c:196), and PBeam runs its `stat` on a dirty IO thread, so a `stat` that waits stops only its caller (see "Dirty NIFs"). The binding has no capability check (row "Capability checks"). `prim_file:read_file_nif/1`, `list_dir_nif/1`, and `read_link_nif/1` give `{error, einval}` for the same paths. Else `read_file_nif/1` opens the bytes before the first NUL with `O_RDONLY`, gives `{error, eisdir}` for a directory, and reads the file to the size that `fstat` gives, or to its end for the size 0 or a failed `fstat` (prim_file_nif.c:1300-1387, unix_prim_file.c:126-226). `list_dir_nif/1` gives the names of `readdir` without `.` and `..` as binaries in the reverse order of `readdir`, and a failed `readdir` ends the list (unix_prim_file.c:897-943). `read_link_nif/1` gives the bytes of `readlink`, with a buffer of 256 bytes that doubles while the contents fill it (unix_prim_file.c:859-895). `get_cwd_nif/0` gives the bytes of `getcwd` (unix_prim_file.c:1050-1083). Each gives `{ok, Binary}` or `{ok, Names}`, or `{error, Errno}` with the ERTS `erl_errno_id` atom (prim_file_nif.c:1115-1147, 1269-1280). For an empty symbolic link, which macOS allows, `readlink` gives 0 bytes and does not set `errno`, so ERTS and PBeam give the `errno` that an earlier call of the dirty IO thread left. PBeam opens a file with `O_CLOEXEC` too, which changes only a child that starts while the read runs. ERTS runs these four NIFs on a dirty IO scheduler (prim_file_nif.c:200-201, 207, 212), and PBeam runs their host calls on a dirty IO thread, so a read that waits stops only its caller (see "Dirty NIFs"). They have no capability check (row "Capability checks"). `prim_file:open_nif/2` raises `error:badarg` for modes that are not a list. It reads the modes `read`, `write`, `exclusive`, `append`, `sync`, `skip_type_check`, and `directory` and ignores each other element; `append` and `exclusive` add `write`, and no `read` and no `write` gives `read` (prim_file_nif.c:500-539). It gives `{error, einval}` for the same paths as `read_info_nif/2`. Else it opens the path with the flags of `get_flags` and the mode `0666`, or `0777` with `directory`, and a directory without `directory` or `skip_type_check` gives `{error, eisdir}` (unix_prim_file.c:142-226). PBeam opens the file with the safe Rust `std::fs::OpenOptions`, which adds `O_CLOEXEC`. The result is `{ok, Ref}` or `{error, Errno}` with the ERTS `erl_errno_id` atom. `Ref` is a reference to a file resource, a host `File` in the `Files` value of the node `Resources`, as a TTY resource is. The resource monitors the caller, as `enif_monitor_process` does, and when the caller exits while the file is open, PBeam closes the file in the exit of the caller, where ERTS sends the close to the process `erts_prim_file` (prim_file_nif.c:362-405, 541-570). `close_nif/1`, `read_nif/2`, `write_nif/2`, and `seek_nif/3` raise `error:badarg` for a term that is not a file resource, also a TTY resource or a `make_ref/0` reference, and give `{error, einval}` for a closed resource before they read the other arguments (`file_handle_wrapper`, prim_file_nif.c:315-357, 618-647). `close_nif/1` removes the monitor of the resource on its owner, as `enif_demonitor_process` does (prim_file_nif.c:633), closes the host file, and gives `ok`. PBeam has no garbage collection of resources, so it keeps the entry of a closed resource, with no file and no owner, until the end of the run, and a later call gives `{error, einval}`, as in ERTS. A failed `close` gives `{error, Errno}` with the ERTS `erl_errno_id` atom. PBeam closes with the safe `nix::unistd::close`, one call with no new call on `EINTR`, as `efile_close` does (unix_prim_file.c:246-265). A close at the exit of the owner ignores the result, as `delayed_close_nif` does (prim_file_nif.c:362-375). `read_nif/2` raises `error:badarg` for a size that is not a number and gives `{error, einval}` for a float, an integer outside the `Sint64` range, or a negative integer. Else it allocates the size, and a failed allocation gives `{error, enomem}`. It then reads until the size is full or a read gives 0 bytes and gives `{ok, Bytes}`, `eof` for 0 bytes, or `{error, Errno}` (prim_file_nif.c:649-686, unix_prim_file.c:292-341). `write_nif/2` reads the first 64 elements of the iovec, and raises `error:badarg` with no write when one of them is not a binary or when the rest after them is not a list (`examine_iovec_term`, erl_nif.c:4107-4166). Else it writes the bytes of the binaries, with no copy, with `writev`, with one `write` of the first binary after `EINVAL` and one `write` of its first 2^31 - 1 bytes after `EINVAL` again, until all are written or a write gives 0 bytes, as `efile_writev` does (unix_prim_file.c:343-394), and gives `ok`, `{continue, Rest}` for a longer list, or `{error, Errno}` (prim_file_nif.c:688-710). `seek_nif/3` raises `error:badarg` for an offset that is not an integer in the `Sint64` range, then for a mark that is not `bof`, `cur`, or `eof`, and gives `{ok, Position}` or `{error, Errno}` of `lseek` (prim_file_nif.c:782-806, unix_prim_file.c:508-540). ERTS runs these five NIFs on a dirty IO scheduler (prim_file_nif.c:179-182, 185), and PBeam runs their host calls on a dirty IO thread, so a read or a write that waits stops only its caller (see "Dirty NIFs"). `prim_buffer:new/0` keeps a buffer resource with a queue, an accumulator, and an external lock in the `Buffers` value of the node `Resources`, next to `Files`, and PBeam keeps each buffer resource until the end of the run. `size/1`, `peek_head/1`, `copying_read/2`, `write/2`, `skip/2`, `find_byte_index/2`, `try_lock/1`, and `unlock/1` give the ERTS result of `prim_buffer_nif.c`: a term that is not a buffer resource raises `error:badarg`, `peek_head/1` gives `error:badarg` for a queue of no bytes (`erl_nif.c:4377-4383`), `find_byte_index/2` gives `{ok, Index}` or `not_found`, `try_lock/1` gives `acquired` or `busy`, and `unlock/1` gives `ok` or raises `error:lock_order_violation`. `write/2` puts the small writes of one call of at most 64 iovec elements into the accumulator and each other element into the queue as its own entry, and an empty element is no queue entry (`combine_small_writes`, `prim_buffer_nif.c:222-248`; `erts_ioq_enqv`, `erl_io_queue.c:258-280`). PBeam runs the write of a longer iovec and the search of a long queue in one call of the NIF, with no `enif_schedule_nif` and no timeslice (`prim_buffer_nif.c:357-361`, `444-458`), and it has no allocation failure path, so `new/0` and the other NIFs never give `out_of_memory` (`prim_buffer_nif.c:270`). They have no capability check (row "Capability checks"). A call to each other function in `-nifs` runs its BEAM stub body. The bindings are active from module load, also before `erlang:load_nif/2` and after a failed load. A `call_ext`, `call_ext_last`, or `call_ext_only` of `erlang:load_nif/2` runs as the `i_load_nif` instruction. PBeam models each module with bindings as an ERTS static NIF library, and only `prim_buffer`, `prim_file`, `prim_tty`, and `zlib` have bindings. It raises `error:badarg` for a file name that ERTS does not accept, with the ERTS file name encoding of the host. From `prim_buffer`, `prim_file`, or `prim_tty`, it then gives the ERTS `reload`, `Function not found`, and `Function not declared as nif` errors in the order of the 9, 33, or 20 `nif_funcs` entries of the library (prim_buffer_nif.c:86-96, prim_file_nif.c:177-219, prim_tty_nif.c:153-174), else `ok`. `zstd`, `prim_socket`, `prim_net` with the library `net`, `erl_tracer`, and `asn1rt_nif` are static libraries with a function table, and of these `prim_socket` has the bindings `nif_supports/0,1`, and `zstd` has the 11 bindings of its contexts, and `asn1rt_nif` has the bindings of its 3 functions. The `asn1rt_nif` bindings port `asn1_erl_nif.c` step by step and match the pinned ERTS for well-formed and malformed input, with three differences: where the C code of `encode_per_complete` reads after the end of its input with no check (opcode 10 as one of the last two bytes, `asn1_erl_nif.c:186-199`), ERTS reads memory outside the term, and PBeam stops the run with the runtime error `asn1rt_nif:encode_per_complete/1 reads after the end of its input at the opcode 10, which PBeam does not support`; `encode_ber_tlv` of a tuple with fewer than two elements gives `{error,-1}`, where the C code reads after the tuple (`asn1_erl_nif.c:1033-1036`); and `decode_ber_tlv_raw` stops the run with a clear runtime error for an input that nests more than 453 values: the C code stops at a stack distance of 8192 pointer words (`asn1_erl_nif.c:954-958`), so the depth of its `{error, {unknown, Index}}` depends on the stack frame size of the build (455 calls on aarch64-apple-darwin, more on x86_64 Linux). From one of these modules, `erlang:load_nif/2` gives the same errors in the order of the 24, 40, 15, 2, or 3 `nif_funcs` entries of the library (zstd_nif.c:1082-1116, prim_socket_nif.c:18696-18748, prim_net_nif.c:4997-5022, erl_tracer_nif.c:49-52, asn1_erl_nif.c:1343-1347), and each call of another function of a table stops the run with the runtime error `M:F/A NIF is not supported`. The decision of 2026-10-22 (#855, #887): PBeam gives the ERTS values of the support query, so `prim_socket:init/0` completes at the node boot (prim_socket.erl:184-204), and each socket operation stays a clear runtime error. `prim_socket:nif_supports/0,1` give the values of the pinned ERTS that `mise.toml` installs on the host OS, macOS or Linux: PBeam keeps the values of the C headers of that build in a table with one column for each OS (prim_socket_nif.c:526-914, 3217-4375, 5272-5687). Neither build has `HAVE_SCTP`, so the values have no SCTP option level, no default protocol `sctp`, and `{sctp, false}`; an OTP build with SCTP gives other values. The protocols come from the host file `/etc/protocols` at each call, as `getprotoent` gives them; PBeam reads only that file and no other source of the name service switch. A key that is not one of the five keys gives `[]`. The order of the elements of each list of `nif_supports/1` is not observable, because `prim_socket` does not export the function and `init/0` folds each list into a map (prim_socket.erl:195-255); PBeam does not sort the tables as ERTS does at load (prim_socket_nif.c:4379-4396). The load callback of `prim_socket` gives the veto 1 for a load info that is not a map with a local pid under the key `registry` (prim_socket_nif.c:18862-18867, socket_util.c:2958-2971), and the load callback of `asn1rt_nif` gives the veto -1 for each load info other than the integer 1 (asn1_erl_nif.c:1320-1329). The load callbacks of the other three return 0. PBeam starts no socket I/O backend and makes no resource type, so it has no veto of `prim_socket` or `prim_net` for a failed start or resource type (prim_socket_nif.c:19161-19201, prim_net_nif.c:5135-5145). From each other module, it opens the NIF shared library of the path (row "NIF shared libraries"). An `apply`, `apply_last`, `erlang:apply/2`, `erlang:apply/3`, or fun call of `erlang:load_nif/2` runs the BIF, which returns the ERTS `bad_lib` error and does not check the file name. `crypto` is a static library of the same kind: `erlang:load_nif/2` from `crypto` checks the 70 functions of the `nif_funcs` table of `crypto.c` in order, and its load callback checks the load info `{302, LibBin, FipsMode}` with the vetoes of `initialize` (`crypto.c:204-218`, `286-288`), so a failed check gives `{error, {load, "Library load-call unsuccessful (Line)."}}`. PBeam has no FIPS mode, and the pinned ERTS has no FIPS support, so `FipsMode` `true` gives the veto 287 as in ERTS (`fips.c:59-60`). A load after the veto 287 loads the library, where the pinned ERTS stops with a segmentation fault. `crypto:strong_rand_bytes_nif/1` reads its bytes from `/dev/urandom` in place of the OpenSSL `RAND_bytes`. The first call opens `/dev/urandom`, and the run keeps it open for each later call. The NIF gives `false` when the open or the read fails, so `crypto:strong_rand_bytes/1` raises `error:low_entropy` where ERTS can give the bytes. It raises `error:badarg` for a term that is not an integer from 0 to `INT_MAX` (`rand.c:26-50`). `crypto:hash_nif/2` computes each digest of `digest.c:25-179` that the OpenSSL 3.5 default provider has with a static OpenSSL, as ERTS does, through the safe `openssl` crate; `md4` raises the `notsup` error tuple of `hash.c:115`, a name that is not a digest type raises the raw error tuple of `hash.c:111`, and data that is not a binary raises the tuple of `hash.c:118`. `crypto:hash_info/1` gives the ERTS map from the fetched digest, so SHAKE gives the size 0 (`hash.c:74-99`, `evp_lib.c:1041`). `crypto:mac_nif/4` computes HMAC, CMAC, and Poly1305 with an OpenSSL `EVP_PKEY` in place of the `EVP_Q_mac` of ERTS, and gives the raw error tuples of `mac.c:188-505`; it runs on the scheduler thread for each text size, where ERTS runs a text of more than 20000 bytes on a dirty CPU scheduler (`mac.c:199-203`). `crypto:hash_equals_nif/2` compares with `CRYPTO_memcmp` (`hash_equals.c:27-53`). `crypto:pbkdf2_hmac_nif/5` gives the error tuple of `pbkdf2_hmac.c:63` for a key length above `INT_MAX` whose C `int` is negative, as ERTS does; a key length that truncates to another size, or a password or salt longer than `INT_MAX` bytes, stops the run with a runtime error where ERTS passes the truncated `int` to `PKCS5_PBKDF2_HMAC` (`pbkdf2_hmac.c:58-61`). On ERTS, a negative C `int` gives the error tuple of line 63, and a multiple of 2^32 (C `int` 0) gives a binary of `KeyLen` uninitialized bytes, where PBeam stops the run with the runtime error. The hash context NIFs keep each context on the node resource store until the end of the run, because PBeam has no garbage collection of resources; ERTS frees an `EVP_MD_CTX` resource when no term refers to it (`hash.c:45-51`) (entry "Free crypto hash contexts with their last term" of `docs/backlog.md`). The MAC context NIFs keep each context the same way; ERTS frees a `mac_context` resource when no term refers to it (`mac.c:544-556`). A second `crypto:mac_final/1` of a Poly1305 context, or a `crypto:mac_update/2` of it after its final that fills a 16-byte block, stops the pinned ERTS, because `Poly1305_Final` clears the context (OpenSSL 3.5.5 `poly1305.c:480-499`); PBeam stops the run with the runtime error `crypto:mac_final_nif/1 on a Poly1305 context after its final is not supported` (or the same text for `mac_update_nif/2`). `crypto:pbkdf2_hmac_nif/5` computes the eligible digests with an HMAC and the PBKDF2 chain of RFC 2898, and raises the raw error tuples of `pbkdf2_hmac.c:36-63`; `Iter` keeps the low 32 bits of the C `int`, and a value of 0 or less gives the error of `pbkdf2_hmac.c:63`. The pinned macOS libcrypto has no MD4 support, so `md4` gives `"md.p is not NULL"` at `pbkdf2_hmac.c:38`; each other known digest that is not eligible gives line 40. ERTS runs a call with `Iter > 100` or `KeyLen > 64` on a dirty CPU scheduler (`pbkdf2_hmac.c:86-99`), and PBeam runs it on the scheduler thread, so a long call stops each other process until it returns. `crypto:strong_rand_range_nif/1` draws the bytes from `/dev/urandom` and draws again while the value is not below the range, as `BN_rand_range` does (`rand.c:54-85`); the range 0 gives `false`, and a term that is not a binary raises `error:badarg`. `crypto:info_nif/0` and `crypto:info_lib/0` give the map and the list of the static OpenSSL build (`info.c:110-204`). PBeam links OpenSSL 3.5.5 (`openssl-src = "=300.5.5+3.5.5"`), because crates.io has no build of 3.5.8, so `crypto:info_lib/0` gives `[{<<"OpenSSL">>,810549328,<<"OpenSSL 3.5.5 27 Jan 2026">>}]` where the pinned macOS ERTS gives 3.5.8 (`810549376`); the pinned ERTS on Linux links OpenSSL 3.0.13 dynamically, and PBeam reports its own static build on each host. The sign and verify NIFs give the results and the error tuples of `pkey.c:212-1122` for the algorithms `rsa`, `dss`, `ecdsa`, and `eddsa`. PBeam builds each ECDSA curve from its definition, as ERTS does after OpenSSL fails to load the curve by name (`ec.c:250-267`); the signatures are the same. PBeam has no engine resource, so a map key gives the error tuple of a failed `get_engine_and_key_id` (`pkey.c:494`, `pkey.c:686`). An ML-DSA or SLH-DSA algorithm, another algorithm atom, or an ECDSA curve over a characteristic-two field stops the run with the runtime error `crypto:F/A NIF is not supported for <part>`. Each other `crypto` NIF stops the run with the runtime error `crypto:F/A NIF is not supported`. An exception of a NIF binding has the stack trace frame `{Module, Function, Args, []}` first, as in ERTS. | `erlang:load_nif/2` loads the NIF library, and each NIF replaces the stub body of its function only after a successful load (`erl_nif.c:4990-5007`). `tty_tigetnum_nif` and `tty_tigetflag_nif` give the bytes of `enif_inspect_iolist_as_binary` to `tgetnum` and `tgetflag` with no NUL added (prim_tty_nif.c:843-846, 854-857), so for iodata with no NUL the C function reads the memory after the bytes. ERTS uses its built-in library for a static NIF module and opens each other library with `dlopen`, which gives `{error, {load_failed, Text}}` with host text when the file does not exist (`erl_nif.c:4751-4787`). The BIF `erlang:load_nif/2`, which `apply` and fun calls reach, returns `{error, {bad_lib, "load_nif/2 must be explicitly called from the NIF module. It cannot be called through apply/3."}}` (`erl_nif.c:4618-4626`). During the OTP boot, `standard_error:server/0` (standard_error.erl:70) and `user_drv:init/1` (user_drv.erl:182) call `prim_tty:load/0` (prim_tty.erl:197-202). `prim_tty:tty_create/1` makes the TTY resource, ERTS frees it when no term refers to it, and `write_nif/2` writes the iovec to its output fd (prim_tty_nif.c:354-427). | PBeam has no garbage collection of resources. `tty_create/1` is not exported, and only `prim_tty:init/1` calls it (prim_tty.erl:220), so the host write is reachable only after the OTP IO servers start. The boot module `pbeam_boot` of `examples/hello_world` starts them, so the host write is reachable when the `pbeam` binary runs the boot (see "Boot"). A read after the end of the bytes is undefined behavior in C, and `prim_tty` always adds a NUL to the code (prim_tty.erl:1405-1408). Task #126 adds the other start-path NIFs, task #127 adds `erlang:load_nif/2`, task #115 adds `tty_select/2` and `tty_encoding/1`, task #134 adds the host write of `write_nif/2`, task #139 adds the boot, task #299 adds the select of `tty_select/2` and `read_nif/3`, task #19 runs `write_nif/2` on a dirty IO thread, task #388 adds `tigetnum_nif/1` and `tigetflag_nif/1`, task #390 adds `prim_file:read_info_nif/2` for `os:find_executable/1` and `file:read_file_info/1`, task #443 adds `read_file_nif/1`, `list_dir_nif/1`, `read_link_nif/1`, and `get_cwd_nif/0` for `erl_prim_loader` (erl_prim_loader.erl:163-214, 721, 1140, 1195, 1712), and task #731 adds `open_nif/2`, `close_nif/1`, `read_nif/2`, `write_nif/2`, and `seek_nif/3` for `file:open/2` and `file:write_file/2`. Task #733 adds the `prim_buffer` NIFs, the read buffer of a file of `prim_file:open/2` with a read mode, or with no `write` mode (prim_file.erl:512-527). Task #777 adds the other `prim_file` NIFs of the `file_server` write and metadata paths. Task #868 adds the function tables of the OTP NIF libraries that the `plug_hello` bundle references and that have no binding, so that their modules load and a call of an unbound function fails with a clear runtime error. `tinfo_nif/0`, `tigetstr_nif/1`, `tputs_nif/2`, and `wcwidth/1` stay unsupported: Rust has no safe terminfo binding, and the project rule forbids new `unsafe` code. A local file of `file:open/2` in write mode reads and writes at once. A `stat` of a local path returns at once. A read of a code file on a local file system also returns soon. The `zlib` bindings run the deflate and inflate streams with the pure-Rust `zlib-rs` crate, and each stream is a reference in the `ZlibStreams` value of the node `Resources`. The compressed bytes can differ from the pinned zlib 1.3.2: for the 25-byte input `<<"hello hello hello world">>` at level 1, pinned ERTS gives `7801cb48cdc9c957c84022cbf38b725200687d08c5` and PBeam gives `7801cb48cdc9c9574026cbf38b725200687d08c5`; for the 45000-byte repeated sentence `The quick brown fox jumps over the lazy dog. ` at level 6 with the `filtered` strategy, the two outputs also differ. A deflate stream with the `sync` and `full` flushes also has different bytes: for `<<"hello hello hello world">>` at level 6 it is `789cca48cdc9c957c84022cbf38b725200000000ffff4216401206000000ffff03009aa51189` on PBeam and `789cca48cdc9c957c84022cbf38b725200000000ffffcac02e0c000000ffff03009aa51189` on the pinned ERTS. `zlib-rs` and the pinned zlib 1.3.2 each decode the stream of the other, and the decompressed bytes are the same. `zlib-rs` has no allocation failure path, so a `zlib` NIF never gives `mem_error` for a failed output allocation. `zlib:deflateSetDictionary_nif/2`, `zlib:deflateParams_nif/3`, `zlib:inflateSetDictionary_nif/2`, and `zlib:inflateGetDictionary_nif/1` have no binding, so a call stops the run with the runtime error `zlib:F/A NIF is not supported`. The `zstd` bindings run libzstd 1.5.7 of the `zstd-sys` crate 2.0.15+zstd.1.5.7, the version that the pinned ERTS vendors (`erts/emulator/zstd/vendor.info`), through the safe API of `zstd-safe`, so the compressed bytes are the bytes of the pinned ERTS for the same input and parameters. Each context is a reference in the `ZstdContexts` value of the node `Resources`, and PBeam keeps it until `zstd:close/1` or the end of the run. The `zstd` dictionary functions, `get_compress_parameter_nif/2`, `get_decompress_parameter_nif/2`, the four `getDictId_*_nif/1` functions, and `get_frame_header_nif/1` have no binding, so a call stops the run with the runtime error `zstd:F/A NIF is not supported`. The safe API of `zstd-safe` takes no strategy value 0, so `zstd:set_parameter(Ctx, strategy, default)` after another strategy stops the run with a runtime error, where ERTS sets the default strategy again (zstd_nif.c:380-382); before another strategy, it gives `ok` as ERTS does. PBeam ignores the reductions of `enif_consume_timeslice` of the `zstd` stream NIFs (zstd_nif.c:644). (#63, #50, #126, #127, #115, #134, #139, #299, #19, #388, #390, #443, #731, #733, #770, #776, #777, #868, #898) The `crypto` algorithm lists of `crypto:supports/0,1` name only the algorithms that PBeam can run: `hash_algorithms/0` gives the 18 digests of `hash_nif/2` and `mac_algorithms/0` the 3 MACs of `mac_nif/4` in the ERTS order, `cipher_algorithms/0` gives the 35 ciphers that the default provider of the static OpenSSL has in the order of their atom indexes (`cipher.c:239`, `395-416`), which is the ERTS order, and the other lists are empty. The types of `cipher_info_nif/1` come from a fixed table (`CIPHER_TYPES` in `src/runtime/interpreter/native/crypto/cipher.rs`) with the values of the macOS OpenSSL 3.5.x, for example 61 for `des_ede3_cfb`; the ERTS of the Linux CI host with OpenSSL 3.0.x gives 30 (`NID_des_cfb64`). The one-shot cipher NIFs give `notsup` for `rc2_cbc`, `rc4`, `des_cbc`, `des_cfb`, `des_ecb`, and the `blowfish` ciphers, as the pinned macOS ERTS does; the libcrypto of the Linux CI host has these legacy ciphers and has no `sm4_gcm` and `sm4_ccm`, which PBeam runs (`cipher.c:216-218`, `api_ng.c:284-287`). The pinned ERTS lists each algorithm of its OpenSSL: 187 items on macOS and 177 with the libcrypto of the Linux CI host (`algorithms.c:86-224`, `digest.c:226-242`), and a caller that reads the list then selects an algorithm. Each binding of an algorithm adds it to its list. `info_fips/0` and `enable_fips_mode_nif/1` give the values of a build without FIPS support, as the pinned ERTS (`fips.c:27-62`). |
| NIF shared libraries | `erlang:load_nif(Path, LoadInfo)` from a module without a static or an extension library opens `Path.so` with `dlopen` and the flags `RTLD_NOW` and `RTLD_LOCAL`, finds `nif_init`, else `_nif_init`, and calls it (erl_unix_sys_ddll.c:107-158, 230-242). It then checks the `ErlNifEntry` in the ERTS order and gives the ERTS result: `{error, {load_failed, Text}}` for a failed `dlopen`, with the `dlerror` text of the host; `{error, {bad_lib, Text}}` for no init function, an init function that returns NULL, a newer or an old NIF version, a vm variant other than `beam.vanilla`, a library name other than the module name, `Function not found`, `Function not declared as nif`, `Illegal flags field value`, and `Duplicate NIF entry`; then `{error, {upgrade, Text}}` for a second instance of the module and a library without an upgrade callback, and `{error, {load, Text}}` for a veto of the load callback (erl_nif.c:4774-4987). Each error closes the library with `dlclose`. PBeam supports only NIF version 2.18 of OTP 29.1.1, so a library of version 2.0 to 2.17, except 2.5, gives `{error, {bad_lib, "That NIF library (Major.Minor) is not supported by PBeam, which supports only NIF version 2.18. Try recompile the NIF lib."}}`. Each executable of PBeam exports only `enif_get_int`, `enif_get_uint`, `enif_inspect_binary`, `enif_get_string`, `enif_make_int`, `enif_make_new_binary`, and `enif_make_badarg` (erl_nif.c:1343-1383, 1463-1473, 1598-1627, 1791-1801, 1843-1873, 1993-2000), the functions of `fixtures/nif/nif_add.c` and `bcrypt_elixir` 3.3.2, so `dlopen` of a library that calls another `enif_*` function gives `load_failed` with the `dlerror` text of the missing symbol. An `ERL_NIF_TERM` is a handle of the terms of one call. A function of the library runs only for the module instance of the load, at its `nif_start` instruction: on the scheduler thread, or with the flags 1 or 2 on the dirty CPU or the dirty IO pool, as ERTS schedules a dirty NIF (erl_nif.c:3437-3477). `enif_get_string` with `ERL_NIF_UTF8` stops the run with a runtime error, and the bytes of a binary of `enif_make_new_binary` are 0 until the NIF writes them. It returns its value, or raises `error:badarg` after `enif_make_badarg` with the frame `{Module, Function, Args, []}` first, and a returned term that no `enif_*` call of the call made stops the run with a runtime error. A function of `-nifs` that the library does not have runs its BEAM stub body. The native code runs in the PBeam process, as in ERTS: a crash, an abort, or a broken C API contract in a NIF stops the process (`docs/adr/0001-unsafe-in-the-nif-abi-layer.md`). An upgrade callback and a module without `nif_start` stop the run with a runtime error. In a run of `execute_on_schedulers`, the node keeps the library of each load, so a process of each scheduler calls it (row "Code loading in a run with scheduler threads"). PBeam does not keep the private data of the load callback, does not call `unload`, and does not add the module to the taint list of `erlang:system_info(taints)`. PBeam does not purge a module, so each library stays open until the end of the run. A byte above 127 in a name of the entry is in the error text as its UTF-8 bytes, and a module name that needs quotes has no quotes in the text.
