Thus other processes on the scheduler run two turns before the caller continues, where ERTS gives two or more. `persistent_term:get/1,2` looks up the key under a short lock of the node, where ERTS reads with no lock. A table copy makes the slots of the new table and copies the old slots within 32 slots for each reduction in each call, as in ERTS. A change with no copy changes the visible table in place when it becomes visible. `persistent_term:erase/1` returns `false` with no switch for a key without a value. It clears the slot when the next slot is free and rehashes the other pairs in old slot order otherwise, as `persistent_term_erase_1` does. It charges one reduction for each 32 slots of a table copy and traps at the end of the budget; PBeam changes the table in the last call, and the charge and the traps are the same. ERTS traps to `persistent_term:erase/1` with a magic reference, and PBeam traps to the internal export `persistent_term:erase_trap/2`, which no Erlang code can call (`erl_bif_persistent.c:275-293`, `432-433`). | At `-eval` time the table already holds 25 terms that `erl_prim_loader`, `code_server`, `os`, the `logger` modules, `erl_features`, `prim_socket`, `prim_net`, `global_group`, and `rpc` put, in a table of 64 slots: `persistent_term:info()` returns `#{count => 25,memory => 32704}`. In this table the keys of the program get other slots, and their relative order can change. For example, the `persistent_term_probe:table/0` fixture puts `true`, `39`, `ok`, and `7`. When you remove the boot terms from its result, the next `get/0` returns `[{39,2},{7,4},{ok,3},{true,1}]` on ERTS and `[{true,1},{7,4},{ok,3},{39,2}]` on PBeam. Their keys contain atoms that are not static, so their order changes from run to run. The ERTS pid data, reference counter, and atom index of a key are different from the PBeam values. An external fun hashes the address of its export entry (`make_internal_hash` in `erl_term_hashing.c:2011-2016`), so its slot changes from run to run: `fun lists:reverse/1` gave 638061310806519221 and 18158401298541009015 in two runs. A process that finds the update permission with another process suspends, and the release resumes it (`erl_bif_persistent.c:333-345`, `467-469`, `692-694`, `1311-1365`). `persistent_term:put/2` of a new value and `persistent_term:erase/1` make the new table visible after thread progress, and suspend the caller until then (`erl_bif_persistent.c:535-539`, `774-779`, `1172-1230`). With `erl +S 1`, other processes then run two or more turns before the caller continues, and the caller sometimes uses 2 to 4 more reductions after it continues. The fixture `fixtures/multischeduler/persistent_terms.erl` gives the same results on `erl +S 1`, `erl +S 2`, and `erl +S 4`. | PBeam does not run the OTP boot. The values of the boot terms contain references, pids, and data of the host socket layer. Even ERTS has no stable slot for these keys. PBeam has no export entry address, so it hashes a word that is equal for equal external funs. (#143) No measured path puts a persistent term while `persistent_term:get/0` traps. (#234) Thread progress depends on all managed threads of the node. PBeam counts scheduling steps of the scheduler of the caller in its place, which keeps the order of the ERTS steps: the caller suspends, others run, the table becomes visible, the permission is released, and the caller resumes. (#245, #932) |
| Map keys | A map key must be a binary, an atom, an integer, a pid, a reference, `[]`, a list or a tuple whose elements are atoms, integers in the ERTS small-integer range, -(2^59) to 2^59 - 1, pids, references, `[]`, or such lists or tuples, such as `{1, 2}` and `[{a}]`, or an improper list whose elements are such keys and whose tail is an atom, a small integer, or such a tuple, such as `[1|x]` and `[1|{2}]`. A hashmap (more than 32 keys) also needs an integer key in the small-integer range. As in ERTS, a flatmap orders two atom keys by atom index and each other two keys in the term order, so an atom in a tuple key compares by name, and a hashmap hashes a binary key with the binary path, a list key, also an improper list key, with the list path, and a tuple key with the tuple path of `make_internal_hash` and keeps two keys with equal hashes in a collision node at level 16, sorted in the term order. A BEAM map literal with binary or tuple keys loads, such as the Elixir `%{"a" => 0, "b" => 0}` and the Erlang `#{{1, 2} => a}`. A BEAM literal loads each integer (`SMALL_INTEGER_EXT`, `INTEGER_EXT`, `SMALL_BIG_EXT`, and `LARGE_BIG_EXT`), also a bignum key of a flatmap. Each other unsupported case returns a runtime error. | A map accepts any term as a key. A hashmap hashes a bignum or compound term with `make_internal_hash` (`erl_term_hashing.c:1769-2255`). It keeps two keys with equal hashes in a collision node at level 16, sorted in the term order (`erl_map.c:2598-2656`, `857-895`). | PBeam has the ERTS internal hash for each term (`src/term/hash.rs`), except the export entry address of an external fun, but `Map` does not support the flatmap key order and the hashmap insertion of bignums, maps, or funs yet, so an improper list key with such a tail, such as `[1|#{}]`, is not supported either. A list or tuple key with a binary element, such as `[<<>>]` or `{<<>>}`, is not supported yet. No current consumer needs these keys. A pid key and a reference key are supported alone and inside a list or a tuple, unless the reference is an alias reference (`fixtures/map/reference_key_probe.erl`, `fixtures/map/pid_key_probe.erl`). (#75, #138, #178, #274, #392, #797) |
| Process dictionary | Each process starts with no table, and its first `put/2` makes a table of 8 buckets. The table grows and shrinks as the ERTS table does, and `get/0`, `get_keys/0,1`, and `erase/0` give the ERTS bucket order (`src/runtime/process/dictionary.rs`). A key gets its bucket from the ERTS `MAKE_HASH`: the value of a small integer, the index of an atom, or else the ERTS internal hash (`src/term/hash.rs`). An atom that is not static has the PBeam atom index. A pid key hashes the PBeam process number, a port key the PBeam port number, a reference key the PBeam reference counter, and an external fun key the word of the persistent terms row. Thus the order is the ERTS order only for keys that contain no pid, no port, no reference, no external fun, and no atom that is not static. | `erl +hpds Size` sets the first bucket count (`erl_process_dict.c:150-156`), with 8 as the default (`erl_init.c:140`). `MAKE_HASH` uses `atom_val` of an atom key (`erl_process_dict.c:58-62`), and the ERTS pid data, port data, reference counter, and atom index are different from the PBeam values. For example, a `tcp_inet` port that ERTS opens and then the integer 6 give `[Port,6]` on ERTS and `[6,Port]` on PBeam. | PBeam has no `+hpds` flag. The atom table, pid, port, reference, and external fun rows give the reasons for the other keys. (#688) |
| Map iteration | `erts_internal:map_next/3` takes the pairs that the reduction budget of the caller permits, as ERTS does: for a hashmap, the budget divided by 4, but at least 1 pair, and at most 32 pairs at path 0, and for an ordered iterator path, the budget divided by 32, but at least 1 key. A hashmap call costs 4 for each pair, and it can make the budget negative. An ordered iterator path costs 32 for each key when the path ends, and else all reductions. A flatmap costs nothing. A BIF that reads a negative budget, through `apply` or as a guard BIF before the next call or return, reads it as its ERTS BIF does: a BIF that keeps the budget signed traps or yields at once, as with a budget of 0, and a BIF that converts it to a `Uint` does all its work in the call. For a path that ERTS returns as a bignum that is not in normal form, or above 2^63 - 1, the BIF returns a runtime error. Such a path has 15 elements and a value below 2^59, or 16 or more elements: the walk stops in a HAMT node at level 14 or below, which only keys whose hashes have the same low 56 bits reach. PBeam returns `badarg` for an invalid ordered iterator path, also when the badarg comes after the first chunk of keys. | ERTS takes `MAX(fcalls / 4, 1)` pairs for a hashmap, at most 32 at path 0, and `MAX(1, reductions / 32)` keys for an ordered iterator path (`erl_map.c:3657-3663`, `3783-3800`). A hashmap call subtracts 4 for each pair from `fcalls` with no lower limit (`erl_map.c:4126`). An ordered iterator path bumps 32 for each key when the path ends, and else all reductions (`erl_map.c:3690-3703`). Each BIF reads a negative budget in its own way: `lists:member/2` traps at once (`erl_bif_lists.c:1114-1130`), and `erlang:length/1` reads it as a large unsigned count (`erl_bif_guard.c:280`). `lists:reverse/2` reads it as a `Uint` (`erl_bif_lists.c:1207`), but the pointer sum wraps, so `lists_reverse_onheap` takes no element, and its `BUMP_REDS` sets the budget to 0 (`bif.h:71-78`). `lists_reverse_alloc` then reads 0 and traps. The results of `maps:to_list/1` and of a `maps:next/1` iteration do not change with the chunk size. For a path of 15 or more elements, ERTS allocates a bignum of `(n + 1) * 4` bits (`erl_map.c:4058-4114`). With 16 elements, its top digit is 0, so on ERTS 29.1.1 the path is not equal to the integer of the same value, and `term_to_binary/1` of it stops the emulator. An invalid ordered iterator path whose badarg comes after the first chunk of keys, such as a 130-key path with a tail that is not `[]`, crashes ERTS 29.1.1 (JIT) with SIGSEGV in about one run in four. The BIF builds one tuple per key in a heap fragment and raises badarg before it sets the last tuple's third element (`erl_map.c:3670-3707`). `HRelease` keeps the half-built tuples in the fragment. The GC after the BIF call copies and scans the whole fragment (`collect_live_heap_frags`, `erl_gc.c`), and the unset element holds uninitialised heap memory that reads as a wild boxed or list pointer. `MallocScribble=1` makes the crash happen on every run. OTP master has the same ordered iterator code. | `maps.erl` calls `erts_internal:map_next/3` only before a call or a return, which switch out the process with a negative budget (`maps.erl:525-542`, `1104-1111`). PBeam has no bignum term (see "Integer range"). No map on the measured start path of #79 has two keys whose hashes have the same low 56 bits. `input_probe:costs/1` gives the same cost as ERTS for flatmaps, hashmaps, and ordered iterator paths (`tests/integration/input_reductions.rs`, #234). `logger_start_probe:negative_budget/0` makes the budget negative before `length/1`, and `logger_start_probe:negative_budget_forms/0` makes it negative before `lists:member/2`, `erlang:'--'/2`, `erlang:iolist_size/1`, `erlang:binary_to_list/1`, and `erlang:processes/0` through `apply`. Both return `ok` on ERTS and on PBeam (`tests/integration/logger_start.rs`, #922). The reference run cannot test the crashing ordered iterator path, so the test `returns_badarg_for_an_ordered_path_that_crashes_pinned_erts` checks only PBeam. This row records the ERTS crash so the PBeam result is not invented. (#155, #258, #922) |
| Map merge reductions | `maps:merge/2` takes a HAMT node that both maps hold with no step, and two copies of one map with no cost, as ERTS does. A map update with `put_map_assoc`, `put_map_exact`, or `maps:put/3` makes a map that holds each node of the old map off the path of the key, as on ERTS. Other maps hold no shared node where ERTS shares one: the result of an update that puts the value that the key already has, and the results of `maps:remove/2`, `maps:take/2`, and `maps:merge/2`, which PBeam builds again. A map in a message to another process, in an ETS table, or in `persistent_term` is the same map as the map of the sender, where ERTS holds a copy. Thus `maps:merge/2` of such maps uses more or fewer reductions than on ERTS. | ERTS takes a node that both maps hold with no step (`erl_map.c:1608-1612`), and returns the first argument when the two arguments are the same term (`erl_map.c:1257-1262`). An update copies only the nodes on the path of the key (`erl_map.c:2477-2503`, `2659-2792`), and returns the same map for a key that already has the same value word (`erl_map.c:2083`, `2219-2222`, `2523-2526`, `2608-2611`). `hashmap_delete` copies only the path of the key (`erl_map.c:2881-3184`), and the merged map holds each node with keys from only one argument (`erl_map.c:1768-1778`). A send to another process and ETS copy the term, and `persistent_term:put/2` copies it to a literal area. Measured on the pinned ERTS with `+S 1` for a hashmap `M` of the keys 1..1000, in reductions of `maps:merge/2`, ERTS then PBeam: `M` and `M`: 0 and 0. `M` and `M#{500 => x}`, also in the other order: 5 and 5. `M` and `M#{2000 => x}`: 5 and 5. `M` and `M` with the keys 1..8 put: 23 and 23. `M` and `maps:put(500, x, M)`, and `M` and `M#{500 := x}`: 5 and 5. `M#{500 => x}` and the same map with `600 => y` put: 9 and 9. `M` and `M` received from a send to the process itself: 0 and 0. `M` and `M#{500 => 500}`: 0 and 5. `M` and `maps:remove(500, M)`: 5 and 213. `M` and `maps:merge(M, #{2000 => x})`: 5 and 213. `M` and `M` sent to another process and back: 213 and 0. | #453 shares the HAMT nodes of a map update only. The other map BIFs build their result with `Map::from_pairs`. A send shares a term by the owner decision R3 of [#450](https://github.com/fahchen/pbeam/issues/450) (`docs/copy-on-write-terms.md`). The `maps_merge` inputs `same`, `self_merge`, and `put` of `input_probe:costs/1` give the same cost as ERTS (`tests/integration/input_reductions.rs`). The backlog entry "Share map nodes in the results of the map BIFs" of `docs/backlog.md` owns the BIF results and the update with the same value. The backlog entry "Model the process heap for GC reductions and the message copy cost" owns the copy of a send, ETS, and `persistent_term`. |
| Atom table | The atom table starts with the 1150 static atoms. Then it holds 17 preloaded atoms that ERTS makes at emulator start and that a PBeam runtime path uses with a fixed name: `unix`, the `inet_drv` atoms `tcp`, `inet_async`, `inet_reply`, `tcp_closed`, `tcp_error`, and `empty_out_q`, the `prim_file` NIF atoms `read`, `write`, `read_write`, `device`, `directory`, `regular`, `symlink`, `other`, and `file_info`, and the `prim_tty` NIF atom `ebadf`. Then it holds the atoms of each module in load order. Each run of `BeamProgram::execute` starts with a copy of this table, and `erlang:list_to_atom/1` puts a new atom into the table of the run, so `BeamProgram::atom` does not find it. `erlang:system_info(os_type)` puts the flavor atom into the table of the run at its first call. PBeam does not create the atoms of emulator start and OTP boot. `BeamProgram::load` loads all modules at the start, in the given order. `erts_internal:prepare_loading/2` puts the atoms of a module that a run loads into the table of the run. A literal that PBeam cannot decode yet does not create its atoms after the unsupported part. Thus an atom that is not static can get a different index than on ERTS. This changes the hashmap order of non-static atom keys and of list keys that hold a non-static atom, and the flatmap order of two atom keys that ERTS creates in a different order. | At `-eval` time the table holds about 10966 atoms on macOS and 10342 on Linux. The atoms from index 1150 depend on the platform and the build. After about index 3740 their order changes from run to run. In interactive mode a module loads at its first call. ERTS makes `unix` and the flavor atom at emulator start (`os_info_init` in `erl_bif_info.c:6418-6440`), the `inet_drv` atoms at driver start (`inet_init` in `inet_drv.c:4554-4574`), and the NIF atoms when the preloaded `prim_file` and `prim_tty` modules load their NIF libraries (`prim_file_nif.c:243-267`, `prim_tty_nif.c:186`, `1267-1268`). A path whose atom ERTS also makes at each call (`enif_make_atom`), for example `enotsup`, an errno name, or a `load_nif/2` error kind, still puts its atom at each call. | PBeam does not run the emulator start or the OTP boot, and it has no code loading on demand. Even ERTS has no stable index for these atoms. (#84, #111) |
| System information | `erlang:system_info/1` supports only the items `os_type`, `otp_release`, `schedulers`, `build_type`, `endian`, `wordsize`, `debug_compiled`, and `system_logger`. `system_logger` returns the system logger of the node (`erl_bif_info.c:3547-3548`). `os_type` returns `{unix, Flavor}`, where `Flavor` is the `uname` system name in lowercase, for example `{unix,darwin}` on macOS. `otp_release` returns the string `"29"`, the major part of the `erlang` pin in `mise.toml`, which `build.rs` reads at build time. `schedulers` returns `1`, the one scheduler of PBeam, as ERTS does with `erl +S 1` (`erl_bif_info.c:3257-3260`). `build_type` returns `opt` (`erl_bif_info.c:2799-2824`). `endian` returns the byte order of the PBeam target, `little` on each pinned host, as ERTS returns the byte order of its build (`erl_bif_info.c:3101-3106`). `wordsize` returns the size of a machine word of the PBeam target, `8` on each pinned host, as ERTS returns `sizeof(Eterm)` (`erl_bif_info.c:3099-3100`). `debug_compiled` returns `false`, as an ERTS build without `DEBUG` does (`erl_bif_info.c:3382-3387`). Each other item returns the runtime error `unsupported erlang:system_info/1 argument ...`, also an item that ERTS rejects with `badarg`. PBeam reads the system name with the Unix `uname` call, so it supports only Unix hosts and always returns the family `unix`. | `system_info/1` returns many other items, and raises `badarg` for an item that it does not know (`system_info_1` in `erl_bif_info.c`). On Windows, `os_type` is `{win32, nt}`. | Only `os:type/0` calls `system_info/1` on the measured start path of #79, from `prim_tty:init_term/1` (`prim_tty.erl:284`). `elixir:parse_otp_release/0` (`elixir.erl:127-129` of Elixir v1.20.4) reads `otp_release` at the start of the `elixir` application, and `elixir:check_endianness/0` (`elixir.erl:33-41`) reads `endian`. `erts_internal:await_sched_wall_time_modifications/2` (`erts_internal.erl:759-761`) reads `schedulers` after `kernel_refc:init/1` calls `erts_internal:scheduler_wall_time(false)`. `init:has_small_memory/0` (`init.erl:1410-1415`) reads `wordsize` and `debug_compiled` at each `primLoad` command of the boot. (#111, #660, #719, #861) |
| System flags | `erlang:system_flag/2` supports the items `system_logger`, `debug_flags`, `backtrace_depth`, and the heap flags `fullsweep_after`, `min_heap_size`, `min_bin_vheap_size`, and `max_heap_size` (row "Heap limit"). `system_logger` takes `logger`, `undefined`, or a local pid, sets the node value, and returns the old value. The start value is `logger`. The value has no other effect, because PBeam writes no error report (see "Error report"). `debug_flags` returns `true`. `backtrace_depth` takes a small integer of 0 or more, sets the depth of the node to at most 64, and returns the old depth. The start depth is 8. The node keeps one value of `system_logger` and of `backtrace_depth`, so on `execute_on_schedulers` each scheduler reads the value that another scheduler sets, as with `erl +S 2`. Each other value raises `badarg`. The depth limits the frames of a stack trace (see "Exception stack trace"). An item that ERTS does not know raises `badarg`, also `time_offset` with a value that is not `finalize`. Each other ERTS item returns the runtime error `unsupported erlang:system_flag/2 item ...`: `multi_scheduling`, `schedulers_online`, `trace_control_word`, `sequential_tracer`, `reset_seq_trace`, `scheduler_wall_time`, `dirty_cpu_schedulers_online`, `time_offset` with `finalize`, `microstate_accounting`, `outstanding_system_requests_limit`, `scheduling_statistics`, `internal_cpu_topology`, `cpu_topology`, `scheduler_bind_type`, and `erts_alloc`. Before the runtime error, each of these items except `reset_seq_trace`, `cpu_topology`, and `scheduler_bind_type` raises `badarg` for a value that ERTS rejects: `multi_scheduling` takes `block`, `unblock`, `block_normal`, or `unblock_normal`, `schedulers_online` and `dirty_cpu_schedulers_online` take a small integer of 1 or more, `trace_control_word` takes an integer from 0 to 4294967295, `sequential_tracer` takes `false`, a pid, or `{Module, State}` with an atom `Module`, `scheduler_wall_time` takes a boolean, `microstate_accounting` takes a boolean or `reset`, `outstanding_system_requests_limit` takes an integer from 1 to 134217727, `scheduling_statistics` takes `disable`, `enable`, or `clear`, `internal_cpu_topology` takes `undefined` or a non-empty proper list of 7-tuples tagged `cpu`, and `erts_alloc` takes a tuple of two atoms and an integer. `reset_seq_trace`, `cpu_topology`, and `scheduler_bind_type` return the runtime error for each value. A value that passes the check but needs the state of the missing subsystem also returns the runtime error, for example a scheduler count above the host count, a `{Module, State}` tracer without a tracer module, an allocator name that is not known, or CPU ids that are out of range or not unique. | ERTS sets each item (`system_flag_2`, `bif.c:4972-5304`), for example `trace_control_word` returns the old word 0. `backtrace_depth` sets the depth of the node to at most `MAX_BACKTRACE_SIZE` (64) and returns the old depth (`bif.c:5108-5115`, `error.h:206-207`). An item value that is not valid raises `badarg`, also a scheduler count above the host count, a tracer module that is not loaded, an allocator name that is not known, a CPU topology that `verify_topology` rejects (`erl_cpu_topology.c:1348-1460`), and a `max_heap_size` value that is not a valid size or map (`erts_max_heap_size`, `erl_gc.c:3824-3872`). `cpu_topology` and `scheduler_bind_type` first send a deprecation warning to the logger, and `scheduler_bind_type` raises `notsup` on a host without CPU binding, such as macOS (`erts_bind_schedulers`, `erl_cpu_topology.c:714-731`). When an error report goes to a system logger pid that is gone, ERTS sets the system logger to `logger` again (`erl_trace.c:2789-2795`). | Only `logger_proxy:init/1` (`logger_proxy.erl:117`) calls `system_flag/2` on the measured start path of #79. #446 adds `backtrace_depth` for the frames of `erlang:raise/3`. The other items need schedulers, a garbage collector, tracing, a time offset service, statistics, a CPU topology, or memory allocators that PBeam does not have. (#155, #446, #960) |
| Process flags | `erlang:process_flag/2` supports the flags `trap_exit`, `async_dist`, `message_queue_data`, `error_handler`, `priority`, `monitor_nodes`, `{monitor_nodes, Options}`, and the heap flags `fullsweep_after`, `min_heap_size`, `min_bin_vheap_size`, and `max_heap_size` (row "Heap limit"). `async_dist` takes a boolean and returns the old value; its start value is `false`, because PBeam has no emulator flag `+pad`. PBeam has no distribution, so `async_dist` has no other effect. `monitor_nodes` and `{monitor_nodes, Options}` take a boolean and the options of `erts_monitor_nodes` (`dist.c:6731-6845`), and return the old count of the node monitor of the caller with the same options. A node monitor never gets a `nodeup` or `nodedown` message, because PBeam has no distribution and so no node connects or disconnects. `message_queue_data` takes `on_heap` or `off_heap` and returns the old value. Its start value is `on_heap`. PBeam has no process heap, so the flag does not change where the messages are kept, and a change sends no adjust signal to the process. `error_handler` takes an atom and returns the old module of the error handler of the process; its start value is the atom `error_handler` (`erts_proc_set_error_handler`, `erl_process.h:2432-2446`). The export entry of a function that a module does not export runs `undefined_function/3` of that module (row "Undefined functions"). `priority` takes `max`, `high`, `normal`, or `low`, returns the old value, and raises `badarg` for each other value; its start value is `normal` (`bif.c:2052-2056`; `erts_set_process_priority`, `erl_process.c:9456-9547`). The new priority applies when the process next goes into the run queue (row "Scheduling"). A flag that ERTS does not know raises `badarg`. Each other ERTS flag returns the runtime error `unsupported erlang:process_flag/2 flag ...`: `scheduler`, `sensitive`, and `save_calls`. Before the runtime error, each of these flags raises `badarg` for a value that ERTS rejects: `sensitive` takes a boolean, `save_calls` takes a small integer from 0 to 10000, and `scheduler` takes a small integer of 0 or more. A `scheduler` value above the scheduler count returns the runtime error. | ERTS sets each flag (`process_flag_2`, `bif.c:2028-2237`). An `off_heap` message queue keeps the messages outside the process heap (`erl_message.c:1022-1100`), and a change to `off_heap` sends the process an `ERTS_SIG_Q_OP_ADJ_MSGQ` signal after thread progress, which its handling counts (`erl_message.c:1007-1019`; `erl_proc_sig_queue.c:6815-6850`). A flag value that is not valid raises `badarg`, also a `scheduler` value above the scheduler count, a `max_heap_size` value that is not a valid size or map, and `{monitor_nodes, Options}` with options that are not `[]` or a valid map (`erts_max_heap_size`, `erl_gc.c:3824-3872`, `erts_monitor_nodes`, `dist.c:6732-6799`). | `logger_olp:init/1` (`logger_olp.erl:172`) sets `message_queue_data` on the measured start path of #79. `process_flag(error_handler, Module)` needs the export entry of a function that a module does not export, which #447 adds (`call_error_handler`, `beam_common.c:1294-1342`). `global_group:init/1` (`global_group.erl:432`) sets `priority` to `max` at the kernel start (#699). The other flags need schedulers, a garbage collector, tracing, or saved calls. (#155, #447, #699) The backlog entry "Model the process heap for GC reductions and the message copy cost" of `docs/backlog.md` owns the message place and the adjust signal. (#258) |
| Stack trimming | A `move` from a Y register to a Y register fails with the runtime error `stack register yN is unset` when the source register has no term. PBeam runs `init_yregs` as `Instruction::Noop`, so a Y register that only `init_yregs` gives a value has no term. | `init_yregs` writes NIL to the Y register, and the `move` copies NIL to the destination register (`emit_init_yregs` in `erts/emulator/beam/jit/arm/instr_common.cpp:424-509`). The BEAM validator lets the stack trimming optimization emit this `move` (`lib/compiler/src/beam_validator.erl:2502-2508`). | No PBeam test or fixture has this `move` yet. The fix is to copy the empty slot to the destination slot. The validator rejects each other use of the destination, as it does for the source. |
| md5 context | A context binary of 96 bytes whose buffered length is above 64 raises `badarg` in `erlang:md5_update/2` and `erlang:md5_final/1`, like a term that is not a context. | `erts_md5_update` and `erts_md5_finish` copy the length from the context and read or write outside the 64-byte buffer of the state (`erl_md5.c:90-99`, `222-243`), which is undefined behavior. ERTS has no check that rejects the term. | PBeam does not reproduce undefined behavior. A context that `erlang:md5_init/0` and `erlang:md5_update/2` make always has a buffered length of at most 64. (#767) |
| md5 binary remainder | A chunked binary whose byte budget ends inside it is flattened into one binary for the remainder of the walk before `erlang:md5/1` traps, and the flattening costs work and memory in the same turn. | ERTS traps at the budget and makes a sub-binary of the rest (`erl_bif_chksum.c:81-89`, `477-483`), without copying the bytes that the walk already read. | The observable digest and trap points are the same; PBeam keeps the join until the walk needs the remainder. (#767) |
| phash2 reductions | `erlang:phash2/1,2` of a term whose hash needs more iterations than the reduction budget completes the hash in one turn. It charges one reduction for each 64 iterations, and the charge clamps at the end of the budget of the caller, so the excess charge is lost. | `make_hash2_helper` saves the walk in a magic reference and traps when its iteration budget ends (`erl_term_hashing.c:817-855`, `943`, `1565-1570`), so the process switches out and continues in the next turn, and each turn charges its iterations. | PBeam does not keep the walk state of the term hash yet. The value of the hash is the same, but ERTS charges the remaining iterations in later turns. (#767) |
| Diagnostics BIFs | `erlang:process_info/1` reads another process that has incoming signals directly, and gives its state without the process-info request, because `ProcessState::info` reads one atom item. `erlang:delete_module/1` raises the same `badarg` for a module with old code but writes no logger report, a load after the delete loads new current code, and each later load stops with `{not_purged, [Module]}` because PBeam cannot purge the old code. `erlang:has_prepared_code_on_load/1` keeps the `-on_load` flag of a reference after `erlang:finish_loading/1` consumes it, and PBeam has no garbage collection to release that entry. A failed write of `erlang:display_string/2` raises `badarg` without the ERTS `error_info` map. The `priority` option of `erlang:alias/1` and the `stdin` device of `erlang:display_string/2` give a runtime error. | `erlang:process_info/1` sends a process-info request to a process with incoming signals (`erl_bif_info.c:1355-1437`). `erlang:delete_module/1` writes an error report before the `badarg` (`beam_bif_load.c:702-708`), and a purge removes the old code. `erlang:alias/1` accepts `priority` (`bif.c:5869-5870`). `erlang:display_string/2` writes the `stdin` device with `TIOCSTI` (`bif.c:4215-4224`), and raises `badarg` with `error_info` when a write fails (`bif.c:4287-4297`). | The ERTS source of each behavior. |
| Module attribute chunk | PBeam reads the `Attr` chunk at the load of a module with the ERTS walk `decoded_size` (`external.c:6041-6540`), which refuses `FUN_EXT` and `LOCAL_EXT` (`external.c:6399-6404`, `6437-6446`). | ERTS reads the same chunk with `erts_decode_ext_size` (`asm_load.c:1086-1091`) and accepts `LOCAL_EXT` when the block hash of the term matches (`external.c:6469-6510`). | The walk of `LOCAL_EXT` needs the block hash of the local node of the emulator, and no compiler writes that tag into an `Attr` chunk. |
